## Country of service
10A.-(1) This article applies to determine, for the purposes of this Law, the country of service provision.
(2) Subject to the provisions of the Thirteenth Schedule, provision of services is deemed to be carried out -
(a) where the person receiving the services ("the recipient") is a relevant taxable person, the recipient's country of residence, and
(b) otherwise, the country of residence of the person providing the services ("the provider").
(3) The place of provision of a right, or of option or priority in relation to the provision of services is the same as the place where the provision of services would be deemed to have taken place if it were made by the provider of the right to the recipient of the right, regardless of whether the right, option or priority has been exercised and in the case of the provision of any benefit arising from any right to services, the place of provision is the same as the place of provision of services to which the right relates, whether exercised or not.
(4) A person is considered a "relevant taxable person" in relation to a supply of services, if the person:
(a) is a taxable person based on the provisions of article 9 of Directive 2006/112/EC;
(b) is a person registered under the provisions of the Act;
(c) is considered a taxable person and recognized for VAT purposes. in a member state other than the Republic,
and the services are received by that person, not exclusively for private purposes.
(5) The Council of Ministers may by decree published in the Official Gazette of the Republic -
(a) amend the Thirteenth Schedule by adding to or deleting from it any description or amending any description specified in it from time to time;
(b) provide for exceptions to the provisions of the first and second paragraphs of subsection (2).

*(13(I)/2010)*

## Chargeback for services received from abroad
11.— (1) When services are provided by a person who belongs to a country other than the Republic under the circumstances applicable by virtue of this paragraph, the provisions of the law shall be applied, as if, instead of being deemed to be the provision of services by that person, it is deemed to be the provision of services by the recipient in the Republic, in the context of or to promote the business carried out by the recipient and the transaction is a taxable transaction.
(2) Subsection (1) applies if –
(a) the recipient is a relevant taxable person; and
(b) the place of provision of the services is the Republic, and
where the services are of any description in Parts I and II of the Thirteenth Schedule, the recipient is registered under the provisions of the Act.
(3) Benefits which by virtue of subsection (1) above are considered to be made by the recipient are not taken into account as benefits made by him when any input tax deduction is determined in his case by virtue of article 21(1).
(4) In the application of subsection (1) above, the provision of services deemed to be performed by the recipient is deemed to have been performed at a time determined in accordance with Regulations for determining the time of provision in cases falling under that subsection.
(5) Subsection (1) does not apply to services described in the Sixth and Seventh Schedules.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 13(I)/2010
 
 
 
 
 4(I)/2011)*

## Chargeback for gas and electricity delivered by persons outside the Republic
11A. When gas is delivered through the natural gas or electricity distribution system:
(a) From a person established outside the Republic, and
(b) received by a person registered pursuant to the provisions of this Law, for the purposes of any business he carries out,
then the provisions of this Law and in particular the provisions that impose VAT. on transactions and the provisions that give taxable persons the right to a deduction of input tax are applied as if the recipient had himself delivered the goods within the Republic within the framework or for the promotion of his business and as if the delivery was a taxable delivery.

*(88(I)/2005)*

## Performance of VAT by the customer for certain transactions
11B.-(1) This article applies to services or services together with goods provided within the framework of the construction, conversion, demolition, repair or maintenance of a building or any civil engineering project.
(2) The services referred to in subsection (1) are considered for the purposes of the First Annex of this Law as taxable transactions of the recipient, as well as of the person who carries them out, as long as the recipient receives the services in the context of exercise or for the promotion of the business he carries out, even if these are capital elements of his business.
(3) For the purposes of subsection (2), the value of the transaction is determined on the basis that no VAT is imposed. in the transaction.
(4) In case -
(a) any person provides services to a person ("to the recipient") at any time;
(b) the services provided fall within the services described in subsection (1); and
(c) the recipient is a taxable person at the time of the transaction and receives the services in the context of carrying out or promoting the business he carries out,
the provisions of this Law and in particular the provisions that impose VAT on transactions and the provisions that give taxable persons the right to deduct input tax apply as if the recipient had himself provided the services or the services together with the goods, within the framework of or for the promotion of his business.
(5) The provisions of this Law that regulate the amount of input tax, which a taxable person is entitled to deduct, apply to the transactions for which, pursuant to the provisions of this article, the VAT rendered by the recipient.

*(16(I)/2012
 
 
 
 
 122(I)/2020)*

## Return of VAT by the recipient for certain supplies of goods
11C.-(1) This article applies to the deliveries of goods made to persons registered in the VAT Register and whose economic activity code is the combined nomenclature code 46771 and consist of used metals and/or scrap metal.
(2) The transactions referred to in subsection (1) are considered for the purposes of the First Annex as taxable transactions of the recipient, as long as the recipient receives the goods in the context of carrying out or for the purposes of promoting the business he carries out, even if they constitute capital elements of his business.
(a) any person delivers goods to the consignee at any time;
(b) the goods delivered fall within the categories of goods referred to in subsection (1), and
(c) the recipient is a taxable person at the time of the transaction and receives the goods in the context of carrying out or for the purposes of promoting the business he carries out,
the provisions of this Law and in particular the provisions imposing VAT. on transactions and the provisions that give taxable persons the right to a deduction of input tax, are applied as if the recipient himself had delivered the goods, within or for the purposes of promoting his business.
(5) The provisions of this Law that regulate the amount of input tax, which a taxable person is entitled to deduct, apply to transactions for which, pursuant to the provisions of this article, the VAT rendered by the recipient.
(6) A person to whom the provisions of this article apply, is obliged for a period of six (6) years, from the date on which the goods referred to in subsection (1) were delivered to the recipient, to keep a file, in which the transactions for which this article applies are recorded separately; the file contains all the data concerning the purchase and delivery of goods, for which the provisions of this article apply.

*(118(I)/2013)*

## Chargeback for delivery of real estate and land
11D. - (1) The provisions of this article apply in the case -
(a) Transfer of a building or part of it and the plot of land transferred together with it or of an undivided ideal share on it, as long as it takes place before the first installation on it;
(b) transfer of possession of a building or part of it and the plot of land that is transferred together with it or of an undivided ideal share on it by virtue of a contract of sale or by virtue of an agreement which expressly provides that the building will be transferred together with the plot of land that is transferred together with it at a future time or by virtue of a lease agreement with the right of redemption, if it takes place before the first installation, for which a duly completed application for the issuance of a planning permit after May 1, 2004,
(c) transfer of possession, transfer of an undivided ideal share, transfer of possession by virtue of a contract or agreement of sale or by virtue of an agreement, which expressly provides that the ownership will be transferred at a future time, or by virtue of a lease agreement with the right to purchase undeveloped building land which is intended for the construction of one or more fixed structures and is carried out in the context of the exercise of an economic activity,
when the transfer is made by the borrower in the context of a loan restructuring process, as well as in the context of a forced transfer process to the lender.
(2) Any case of delivery of goods from those provided for in subsection (1), for the purposes of the First Annex, is considered as a taxable transaction of the recipient as well as of the person who carries it out, as long as the recipient receives the goods in the context of exercising or promoting the business that he carries out, even if these constitute capital elements of the recipient's business.
For the purposes of this subsection, the value of the transaction is determined on the basis of the non-imposition of VAT. in the transaction.
(3) In a case where -
(a) A taxable person at any time supplies goods to a person (the "recipient");
(b) the goods delivered come within the meaning of goods in subsection (1); and
(c) the recipient at the time of the transaction is a taxable person and receives the goods in the context of carrying out or promoting the business he carries out,
the provisions of this Law and in particular the provisions that provide for the imposition of VAT on transactions and the provisions that grant a taxable person the right to a deduction of input tax, are applied as if the recipient himself had delivered the goods in the context of or to promote the business he carries out.
(4) The provisions of this Law which provide for the amount of input tax which a taxable person is entitled to deduct, apply to transactions for which the VAT pursuant to the provisions of this article, is assigned by the recipient.

*(157(I)/2017)*

## Performance of VAT by the consignee for certain deliveries of goods
11E.-(1) This article applies to deliveries of goods made to taxable persons. (2) The transactions referred to in subsection (1) are considered for the purposes of the First Schedule as taxable transactions of the recipient, as long as the recipient receives the goods in the context of exercising or for the purposes of promoting the business he carries on, regardless of whether these are capital elements of his business.
(b) the goods delivered fall under the categories of goods-
(i) mobile telephones, i.e. devices manufactured or adapted for use on a recognized network and operating on comparable frequencies, whether or not used for other purposes;
(ii) integrated circuit devices such as microprocessors and central processing units prior to their incorporation into end-user products;
(iii) game consoles, computer tablets and laptops;
(c) the recipient is a taxable person at the time of the transaction and receives the goods in the context of exercising or for the purposes of promoting the business he carries out, the provisions of this Law that regulate the amount of input tax, which the taxable person is entitled to deduct, apply to the transactions for which, pursuant to the provisions of this article, the VAT rendered by the recipient.
(5) A person to whom the provisions of this article apply, is obliged for a period of six (6) years from the date on which the goods referred to in subsection (1) were delivered to the recipient, to keep a record in which the transactions to which this article applies are recorded separately:
Provided that, the file contains all the data concerning the purchase and delivery of goods, for which the provisions of this article apply.

*(122(I)/2020)*

## Chargeback for unwrought and semi-wrought precious metals
11F.-(1) The provisions of this article apply to deliveries of raw and semi-finished precious metals, excluding deliveries governed by the provisions of articles 40 and 42A.
(2) The deliveries of goods provided for in subsection (1) are considered for the purposes of the First Annex as taxable transactions of the recipient and the person who carries them out, as long as the recipient receives the goods in the context of carrying out his business or to promote the business he carries out, even if they constitute capital elements of the recipient's business.
(4) In a case where-
(a) a taxable person supplies goods to a person ("the recipient") at any time;
(b) the goods delivered fall within the goods referred to in subsection (1); and
(c) the recipient is a taxable person at the time of the transaction and receives the goods in the course of his business or in furtherance of his business;
the provisions of this Law and in particular the provisions imposing VAT. on transactions and the provisions that give taxable persons the right to a deduction of input tax are applied as if the recipient himself had delivered the goods in the context of carrying out his business or to promote the business he carries out.
(5) The provisions of this Law which regulate the amount of input tax which a taxable person is entitled to deduct, apply to the transactions for which, pursuant to the provisions of this article, the VAT rendered by the recipient.
(6) A person who is subject to the provisions of this article shall keep a record book of purchases of second-hand items, as defined in the Cyprus Precious Metals Marking Organization Law, for a period of six (6) years.

*(152(I)/2022)*

## Place where the service provider or service recipient belongs
12.-(1) This section applies to determine for the purposes of section 10A or the Thirteenth Schedule or section 11, in relation to any supply of services, whether the supplier or recipient belongs to one country or another.
(2) A person who is a relevant taxable person is deemed to belong to the relevant country.
(3) In subsection (2) the expression “relevant country” means –
(a) if the person has a business establishment or other permanent establishment in a country, and has no such establishment in another country, that country,
(b) if the person has a business establishment, or other permanent establishment or establishments in more than one country, the country in which the relevant establishment is situated, and
(c) otherwise, the person's country of habitual residence.
(4) In paragraph (b) of subsection (3), the expression “relevant establishment” means any business establishment or any of the person's permanent establishments most directly connected with the supply.
(5) A person who is not a relevant taxable person is deemed to belong to the country where he has his habitual residence.
(6) For the purposes of this section, "ordinary residence", in relation to a body corporate, means the place where it is legally incorporated.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 13(I)/2010
 
 
 
 
 4(I)/2011)*

## Scope of VAT on acquisitions from member states
12A.-(1) VAT is imposed on any acquisition of any goods from another Member State when -
(a) the acquisition is a taxable acquisition and takes place within the Republic;
(b) the acquisition is not in accordance with a taxable transaction; and
(c) the person making the acquisition is a taxable person or the goods are subject to excise duty or new means of transport.
(2) The acquisition of goods from another member state is a taxable acquisition if -
(a) falls within the provisions of subsection (3) or the goods relate to new means of transport; and
(b) is not an exempt acquisition.
(3) The acquisition of the goods from another member state falls under the provisions of this subsection if -
(a) the goods are acquired in connection with or in furtherance of-
(i) of any business carried on by any person; or
(ii) any activities carried on other than those carried on in the form of a business by any legal person or any club, association or other association of persons without legal personality;
(b) the person acquiring the goods is the person carrying on the business or, as the case may be, those activities; and
(c) the supplier -
(i) is a taxable person in another Member State at the time of the transaction pursuant to which the goods are acquired; and
(ii) by participating in this act, he acts within the framework of or to promote the business he carries out.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Concept of acquiring goods from another member state
12B.-(1) Subject to the following provisions of this article, references in this Law to the acquisition of goods from another member state shall be interpreted as references to any acquisition of goods for the execution of an act in relation to which the following conditions are met, namely -
(a) the act is a delivery of goods, including anything considered for the purposes of this Law as a delivery of goods; and
(b) the act involves the transfer of the goods from another member state;
and references in this Act to the supplier shall, in relation to such acquisition, be construed accordingly.
(2) It is not essential for the purposes of subsection (1) whether the transfer of the goods from another Member State is carried out by or under the instructions of the supplier or by or under the instructions of the person acquiring the goods or any other person.
(3) Where the person in possession of any goods is not changed, in consequence of anything which is deemed for the purposes of this Act to be a delivery of goods, such delivery shall be deemed for the purposes of this Act to be an act whereby there is an acquisition of the goods by the person making it.
(4) Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may provide with respect to any description of act that the acquisition of goods in accordance with an act of that description is not considered for the purposes of this Law as the acquisition of goods from another member state.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Time of acquisition
12C.-(1) Subject to article 13B and any Regulations issued pursuant to subsection (3), when goods are acquired from another member state, the acquisition is deemed for the purposes of this Law to have taken place on whichever of the following dates is earlier -
(a) on the fifteenth (15th) day of the month following that during which the event occurs which, in relation to such acquisition, is the first relevant event for the purposes of taxation of the acquisition; and
(b) on the day of issuing an invoice, in relation to the act according to which the goods are acquired, of such description as may be determined by Regulations.
(2) For the purposes of this Law, the event which is in relation to the acquisition of goods from another Member State, the first relevant event for the purposes of taxation of the acquisition is the initial transfer of the goods which is related to the act in accordance with which the goods are acquired.
(3) Regulations made by the Council of Ministers may provide for the time at which the acquisition is deemed to take place in specified cases where the whole or part of any consideration contained in the deed for the performance of which the goods are acquired is specified or payable periodically, or from year to year, or at the end of the period. The Regulations may also provide, in relation to any case to which they apply, that goods are deemed to be acquired separately and successively at specified times or intervals.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Place of acquisition
12D. - (1) Subject to article 13B, this article is applied to determine, for the purposes of this Law, whether goods acquired from another member state are acquired within the Republic.
(2) Goods are considered to be acquired within the Republic if they are acquired for the execution of an act which entails their transfer to the Republic and does not entail their transfer from the Republic, and, subject to the provisions of this article, otherwise they are considered to be acquired outside the Republic.
(3) Subject to the provisions of subsection (4), goods are considered to be acquired within the Republic if they are acquired by a person who, for the purposes of their acquisition, uses the number assigned to him for VAT purposes, in the Republic.
(4) Subsection (3) does not require that any goods be considered as acquired within the Republic when it is proven, in accordance with Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers for the purposes of this article, that the VAT -
(a) has been paid to another Member State on the acquisition of those goods; and
(b) has become due under provisions of the law of that Member State which correspond in relation to that Member State to the provisions of subsection (2).
(5) Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may provide for the purposes of this article -
(a) for the circumstances in which a person is deemed to have been allocated a VAT number. in the Republic;
(b) for the circumstances in which a person is deemed to be using such number for the purposes of acquiring any goods; and
(c) for the refund, in specified circumstances, of VAT. paid to the Republic on the acquisition of the goods in respect of which the conditions specified in subsection (4)(a) and (b) are satisfied.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Acquisitions from persons belonging to other Member States
12E. - (1) Subject to subsection (3), when -
(a) a person (hereinafter referred to as "the original supplier") delivers goods to a person belonging to another Member State (hereinafter referred to as "the intermediate supplier");
(b) such delivery involves the transfer of the goods from another Member State and their transfer to the Republic but does not involve the transfer of the goods from the Republic;
(c) both this delivery and the transfer of the goods to the Republic are for the purposes of making a delivery from the intermediary supplier to another person (hereinafter referred to as "the customer" registered under this Law;
(d) none of those supplies involves the transfer of the goods from a Member State to which the intermediary supplier is a taxable person at the time of the transfer without also involving the prior transfer of the goods to that Member State; and
(e) there would be a taxable acquisition by the customer if the delivery to him involved the transfer of the goods from another member state to the Republic,
the delivery from the original supplier to the intermediate supplier is not taken into account for the purposes of this Law and the delivery from the intermediate supplier to the customer is considered for the purposes of this Law, except for the purposes of Part III of the First Schedule, as if it involved the transfer of the goods from another Member State to the Republic.
(2) Subject to subsection (3), when -
(a) a person belonging to another member state carries out such a delivery of goods to a person registered pursuant to this Law, which also entails their installation or assembly at a place in the Republic to which they are transferred; and
(b) it would be a taxable acquisition by the registered person if that supply were treated as if it were not a taxable transaction but as if it involved the transfer of the goods from another Member State to the Republic,
such delivery shall be deemed to be such except for the purposes of Part III of the First Schedule.
(3) Neither subsection (1) nor subsection (2) shall apply in relation to any delivery unless the intermediary supplier or, as the case may be, the person making the delivery complies with such requirements in respect of the production, either before or after the delivery is made, of invoices and other documents, and the provision of information, to -
(a) the Commissioner, and
(b) the supplier,
as determined in Regulations that may be issued by the Council of Ministers. These Regulations may also provide for the time in which, and the type and manner in which, any document will be presented or information will be provided and for the information they will contain.
(4) Where the provisions of this section render a taxable acquisition as having taken place, then subject to section 13B and any Regulations issued under section 12C(3), the acquisition shall be deemed to take place on the day of issue of an invoice, in respect of the transaction for the execution of which the goods are acquired, of such description as may be specified in the Regulations.
(5) For the purposes of this article, a person belongs to another Member State if -
(a) does not have a business or other permanent establishment in the Republic and does not have his habitual residence in the Republic;
(b) is not registered under this Law nor is it required to be so registered;
(c) does not have a VAT representative. and is not currently required to designate such. and
(d) is a taxable person in another Member State;
but, in determining for the purposes of paragraph (b) whether a person is required to be registered under this Act, no account shall be taken of any transactions which, if he were a member of another Member State and complied with the requirements set out in subsection (3), would not be taken into account under this section.
(6) Without prejudice to Article 12D(4), when -
(a) any goods acquired from another Member State in a case which corresponds, in relation to another Member State, to the case specified in relation to the Republic in subsection (1); and
(b) the person acquiring the goods is registered under this Act and would be the intermediary supplier in relation to that relevant case,
the delivery of those goods to that person and the delivery of those goods by that person to the person who would be the customer in that respective case are disregarded for the purposes of this Act, except for the purposes of the information provisions referred to in section 3A.
(7) References in this article to a taxable person in another Member State do not include references to a person who is such a taxable person only by virtue of provisions of the law of another Member State corresponding to the provisions of this Law by virtue of which a person who is not registered in accordance with this Law is considered a taxable person if he is required to be registered.

*(95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 141(I)/2007)*

## Application of customs legislation
13.—(1) Subject to such exceptions and adaptations as may be established by Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers and except in cases where a contrary intention appears, the provisions of customs legislation and other enactments and secondary legislation generally in force from time to time in relation to import duties, excise duties and excise duties levied on the importation of goods into the Republic shall apply to the extent which are relevant in relation to any VAT. chargeable on the importation of goods from a place outside the Member States as applied in relation to any such import duty or excise duty or excise duty or, as the case may be, Community customs duties.
(2) The following provisions are exempted from those applicable as referred to in subsection (1) above, namely-
(a) Any provisions of the Customs Duties and Excise Tax Law in force from time to time that provide for exemption or refund of customs duties or consumption taxes;
(b) any provisions of the customs legislation for the time being in force which provide for the exemption or refund of customs duties or excise duties or excise taxes other than the exemptions provided for in the regulations in force for the tax exemptions of travelers from third countries and in the regulations for tax exemptions granted on the importation of goods in the form of non-commercial small parcels from third countries;
(c) any provision or arrangement granting exemption from the payment of duties and taxes on the importation of goods to any contractor of public works.
(3) The customs formalities for items transported by post and subject to control by the Customs Department and determined by Cabinet Regulations issued in accordance with Article 19 of the Postal Law may provide for special regulations in relation to VAT.
(4)(a) Regulations issued pursuant to this article may provide for special arrangements for the declaration and payment of VAT. when importing goods, excluding products subject to excise duty, with parcels of internal value not exceeding one hundred and fifty euros (€150), as long as the special regime provided for in article 42H is not used.
(b) The special arrangements may allow the person presenting the goods to customs on behalf of the person for whom the goods are intended in the territory of the Community to make use of special arrangements for the declaration and payment of VAT. on importation in respect of goods whose dispatch or arrival is completed in the Republic.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 141(I)/2007
 
 
 
 
 181(I)/2021)*

## Tax warehouse VAT
13A.-(1) The Registrar or any authorized person may, if he deems it appropriate, following a relevant application by or on behalf of any person, approve any registered person as a VAT tax depositary. Such application shall be submitted in writing in such form, accompanied by such information and subject to such conditions as the Commissioner may determine in a Notice published in the Official Gazette of the Republic.
Provided that, the Minister of Finance by his decision may from time to time determine license fees for VAT tax warehouses. as well as their annual operating fees.
(2) Subject to the conditions and Regulations issued pursuant to article 13F, such a person is entitled to maintain a VAT warehouse.
(3) The Commissioner or an authorized person may, when examining an application submitted by a person to become a VAT tax depositary. to take into account any matter which it considers relevant and may, without affecting the generality of that provision, take into account all or any of the following -
(a) the person's compliance history and ability to comply with the obligations arising from this Law and the Regulations issued pursuant thereto;
(b) the person's compliance record and ability to comply with customs legislation;
(c) the person's compliance record and ability to comply with Community customs provisions;
(d) the person's compliance history and ability to comply with the VAT obligations of other Member States. and taxes of equivalent effect to excise duties;
(e) if the applicant is a company, the history of compliance and the ability to comply with the matters referred to in the provisions of paragraphs (a) to (d) of the directors of the company, persons connected with the directors and managing directors of the company. and
(f) if the applicant is a natural person, the compliance history and ability to comply with the matters referred to in paragraphs (a) to (d) of any company of which he is or has been a director or managing director;
and for the purposes of paragraphs (e) and (f) “connected” has the meaning assigned to that term in paragraph 1(4) of the Fourth Schedule.
(4) Subject to the provisions of subsection (5), a person approved pursuant to subsection (1) remains a VAT warehouse. until he ceases to be a registered person or until he notifies the Registrar or an authorized person in writing that he will cease to be a VAT custodian.
(5) The Superintendent or an authorized person may, if he deems it appropriate from time to time-
(a) to impose conditions on the VAT warehouse. in addition to those conditions, if any, which were imposed under subsection (1) and to modify or cancel any conditions previously imposed;
(b) revoke the approval of any person as a VAT warehousing agent; and
(c) revoke the VAT warehouse status. from any premises or places.
(6) Any approval by the Commissioner or authorized person pursuant to subsection (1) and any withdrawal of approval or other action by the Commissioner or authorized person pursuant to subsection (5) shall be notified by him in writing to the VAT tax depositary. and effective on the date of service of the notice or such later date as is specified for that purpose in the notice.
(7) Without prejudice to the provisions of article 32 in relation to the obligation to pay VAT, in subsections (1) and (2) "registered person" includes any legal person which by virtue of that article is considered as a member of a group from time to time.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Goods placed in a tax warehouse VAT: exemption
13B.-(1) Subsections (3) and (4) apply when -
(a) goods are acquired from another member state;
(b) these goods are qualifying goods;
(c) either
(i) the acquisition takes place while the goods remain in VAT storage; or
(ii) after the acquisition but before the delivery, if any, of these goods which follows the person who acquires the goods takes actions so that they are subject to a VAT warehousing regime; and
(d) the person who acquires the goods, not later than the time of acquisition, prepares and keeps a certificate that the goods are placed in tax storage, or, as the case may be, that he will act in such a way that the provisions of paragraph (c)(ii) are met, and the certificate is in such form and is kept for such period as Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may specify.
(2) Subsections (3) and (4) also apply where—
(a) delivery of goods takes place;
(b) the goods in question are qualifying goods;
(c) either -
(i) this supply takes place while the goods remain in the VAT status. storage; either
(ii) after such delivery but before the delivery, if any, of the following goods in question, the person to whom the previous delivery is made takes steps to place the goods under a VAT warehousing regime;
(d) in case it falls under the provisions of paragraph (c)(ii), the person to whom the delivery is made delivers to the supplier, not later than the time of delivery, a certificate in such form as Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may specify that he will act in such a way that the provisions of paragraph (c)(ii) are met; and
(e) the delivery is not an act of retail trade.
(3) The acquisition or delivery in question shall be deemed for the purposes of this Law to have taken place outside the Republic if any subsequent delivery of those goods takes place while the goods remain in tax warehousing.
(4) Where subsection (3) does not apply and the said acquisition or delivery, for the purposes of this Law, is deemed to take place in the Republic, such acquisition or delivery is deemed for the purposes of this Law to take place when the goods are transferred from the VAT storage regime.
(5) When -
(a) subsection (4) applies to an acquisition or delivery;
(b) the acquisition or supply is taxable and not subject to the zero rate; and
(c) the person acquiring the goods or the supplier is not a taxable person but would be so if the provisions of paragraph 1(7) of Part I, paragraph 1(7) of Part II and paragraph 1(6) of Part III of the First Schedule, or any of those provisions, did not apply,
the F.P.A. is levied on such acquisition or supply regardless of whether the person acquiring the goods or the supplier is not a taxable person.
(6) In this article "entitled goods" means goods-
(a) of a description falling under the Eleventh Schedule;
(b) on which any import duties, as defined in Article 4(10) of the Community Customs Code, have either been paid or have been suspended by virtue of Article 224 of that Code or Regulations made under Customs legislation.
(c) in the case of goods imported from a place outside the Member States, on which any VAT is chargeable. by virtue of article 5(1)(c) of this Law it has either been paid or its payment has been suspended in accordance with the Community customs provisions; and
(d) in the case of goods subject to excise duty, on which that excise duty has either been paid or the payment of which has been suspended by virtue of the Excise Law in force from time to time.
(7) For the purposes of this section, notwithstanding subsection (4), an acquisition or delivery shall be deemed to take place at the material time of the acquisition or delivery.
(8) The Council of Ministers may, by Decree published in the Official Gazette of the Republic, amend the Eleventh Annex by adding or deleting from it any goods or by modifying any description thereof.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Tax warehouses VAT: services
13C.-(1) Provision of services is subject to the zero rate when -
(a) the taxable person performs provision of specified services;
(b) these services are wholly performed on or in relation to goods while the said goods remain in a VAT warehousing regime.
(c) except when the services are provided by the VAT warehouse. for VAT storage of the goods, the person to whom the services are provided, delivers to the supplier a certificate, in such a form as Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may specify, that the services are performed in this way;
(d) the supply of services would, but for this section, be taxable and not subject to the zero rate; and
(e) the supplier issues to the person to whom the service is provided, an invoice of such description as may be specified in Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers.
(2) If the supply of services is subject to zero-rating under subsection (1) (hereinafter referred to as "the zero-rated supply of services") then, unless there is a delivery of the goods in question for which the essential time is -
(a) while the goods remain in VAT storage; and
(b) after the essential time for the zero-rated provision of services;
subsection (3) applies.
(3) Where this subsection applies—
(a) the provision of services identical to the zero-rate provision of services is considered for the purposes of this Law to be provided, at the time the goods are moved from the VAT storage regime. or if earlier, at the time any excise duty or excise duty becomes chargeable for the purposes of the person's business to the person to whom the zero-rated supply of services has actually been provided and has been provided by that person in the course of or in furtherance of his business,
(b) this supply has the same value as the zero-rated supply of services;
(c) that benefit is a taxable and not a zero-rated benefit; and
(d) the VAT is imposed on this supply even if the person deemed to be making it is not a taxable person.
(4) In this section "specified services" means -
(a) services of the VAT warehouse tax warehouse. for the safekeeping of the said goods in a VAT storage or tax storage regime∙
(b) in relation to goods subject to a fiscal storage regime, services that perform any physical function on the goods, other than any prohibited by virtue of Regulations issued pursuant to article 13F, such as, and without affecting the generality of the following words, maintenance and repackaging functions.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Move from save: Account rendering
13D.-(1) This section applies to any transaction to which section 13B(4) or 13C(3) applies, i.e. a transaction deemed to take place during the movement or the time any excise duty or excise duty becomes chargeable and any acquisition to which section 13B(5) applies i.e. an acquisition deemed to take place during the movement when the person acquiring the goods is not taxable person.
(2) Subject to any Regulations under subsection (3), any VAT payable. on the transaction or acquisition is paid-
(a) at the time the transaction or acquisition is deemed to take place under that section; and
(b) by the person from whom the goods are moved or, as the case may be, together with the excise duty, by the person liable to pay such excise duty.
(3) Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may provide for the possibility for a taxable person to pay VAT. who is liable to pay under subsection (2) above at a time later than that provided for by different provisions for different descriptions of taxable persons and for different descriptions of goods and services.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Deficits in goods placed in a VAT warehouse.
13E.-(1) This article applies when goods have been subject to a VAT storage regime. and, before they have been lawfully moved from a VAT warehouse, they have been found to be lost or deficient.
(2) In any case where this article applies, unless the Commissioner or an authorized person is satisfied that the absence or deficiency in these goods can be attributed to physical loss or deterioration or another acceptable cause, the Commissioner or authorized person may demand that the tax warehouse VAT. pay directly in relation to the lost goods or to all or part of the deficit, as he deems appropriate, the VAT. which would be imposing.
(3) In subsection (2), "VAT which would be chargeable" means VAT which would be chargeable on the delivery of the lost goods, or of the amount of goods in which the goods are deficient, at the time immediately before the absence or deficiency would have occurred, if the value of that delivery was its normal value but where such time cannot be determined to the satisfaction of the Commissioner or authorized person, this VAT. will be the greater of the VAT amounts. which would be enforceable upon the delivery of the goods in question -
(a) if the value of that supply was its highest normal value during the period (hereinafter referred to as "the relevant period") beginning when the goods were placed in VAT storage; and expires when the absence or deficiency came to the knowledge of the Commissioner or the authorized person; or
(b) if the VAT rate on delivery was the highest rate applicable on the delivery of such goods during the relevant period and the value of such delivery was its highest normal value while that rate prevailed.
(4) This article applies without prejudice to any sanction arising under another provision of this Law or Regulations issued on the basis of the Law.

*(95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 38(I)/2008)*

## Article 13A to 13E: Supplementary
13F.-(1) In articles 13A to 13E and in this article -
"entitled goods" has the meaning assigned to that term by section 13B(6) ∙
"VAT tax warehouse" means a place approved by the Commissioner under section 13A(3) and from which such substance has not been withdrawn;
"VAT tax depositor" means a person approved under section 13A(1) ∙
"essential time" -
(a) in relation to any acquisition or delivery the time of which is fixed in accordance with Regulations under section 9(14) or 12C(3), means such time as may be fixed for the purpose of this section under those Regulations;
(b) in relation to any other acquisition, means the time of arrival of the goods at the destination for which they left that Member State;
(c) in relation to any other supply of goods, means the time at which the supply would be deemed to have taken place under section 9(2) but for subsection (2)(c) of that section; and
(d) in relation to any other provision of services, means the time during which the services are performed;
(2) Any reference in articles 13A to 13E or in this article to goods subject to a VAT storage regime. is, subject to any Regulations issued pursuant to subsection (8)(e), a reference to eligible goods held in a VAT warehouse. or transferred between VAT tax warehouses. in accordance with such Regulations and any reference to the movement of goods from a VAT storage regime. interpreted accordingly.
(3) Subject to subsection (2), any reference in articles 13C and 13D to goods subject to a VAT storage regime. is a reference to goods stored in a VAT warehouse. or transferred between VAT tax warehouses, either the same or in different Member States, without the payment to a Member State of any amount of VAT. and references to movement of goods from VAT storage status. interpreted accordingly.
(4) When as a result of operation on goods subject to a VAT storage regime. their nature changes but the goods resulting from said operation are also entitled goods, the provisions of articles 13B to 13E and this article apply as if the goods resulting from said operation were the original goods.
(5) When as a result of operation on goods subject to a VAT storage regime. these cease to be entitled goods when they cease to be such, articles 13B to 13E apply as if they had at that time been moved from the VAT warehousing regime. and for that purpose the owner of the goods shall be deemed to have been the person who moved them.
(6) When -
(a) any person ceases to be a VAT custodian; or
(b) any premises that cease to have the status of a VAT warehouse,
sections 13B to 13E and this section apply as if the goods for which he is a VAT warehouse, or the goods in the VAT warehouse, as the case may be, had at that time been transferred from the VAT warehouse. and for that purpose the owner of the goods shall be deemed to have been the person who moved those goods.
(7) Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may cover the storage, maintenance, security and processing of goods in a VAT warehouse, and the movement of goods from a VAT warehouse.
(8) Without affecting the generality of subsection (7), Regulations may include provisions -
(a) in relation to -
(i) goods which are subject to, have been subject to or are to be subject to a VAT warehousing regime.
(ii) other goods which are kept, have been kept or are going to be kept in a VAT storage regime;
(iii) premises or places of VAT tax warehouse; and
(iv) VAT tax warehouses. and their businesses,
for the keeping, preservation and bringing of records and the submission of declarations and the provision of information by VAT tax repositories. and from any other persons;
(b) require goods stored in a VAT warehouse. to be brought or made available for inspection by an authorized person when required;
(c) prohibit the execution of VAT on warehouse goods. of such functions as may be determined by the Regulations;
(d) regulate the transfer of goods from a VAT warehouse. to another.
(e) in respect of goods which, although held in a VAT warehouse, are not eligible goods or are not intended by the relevant person to be goods in respect of which exemptions are enjoyed by virtue of articles 13A to 13E and this article;
(f) to prohibit the VAT escrow. from allowing such goods to be moved out of the VAT warehousing regime. without paying any VAT payable. by virtue of article 13D against or with reference to that movement and, in case of violation of this prohibition allows the Commissioner or an authorized person to be so moved making jointly and severally both the tax depositary VAT. as well as the person who moves the goods, responsible for the payment of VAT,
and these may contain such incidental or supplementary provisions as the Council of Ministers deems necessary or expedient.
(9) Regulations may provide for a different provision for different cases, including a provision for different VAT tax warehouses. or different descriptions of VAT warehouses, for VAT warehouses. of different descriptions or for goods of different classes or descriptions or of the same class or description on different occasions.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Value of deliveries of goods or services
14.—(1) For the purposes of this Act the value of any supply of goods or provision of services shall be determined, unless otherwise provided by or under this Act, in accordance with this article and Part I of the Fourth Schedule, and for those purposes subsections (2) to (4) below shall apply subject to the provisions of that Schedule.
(2) If the transaction is carried out for monetary consideration, its value is taken to be such amount as, with the addition of chargeable VAT, is equal to the consideration, including any subsidy directly linked to the price of the transaction.
(3) If the transaction is carried out against a non-monetary or non-entirely monetary consideration, its value is taken to be the amount of money that, with the addition of the applicable VAT, is equivalent to the consideration.
(4) Where a supply of goods or provision of services is not the only thing to which a monetary consideration relates, the transaction is deemed to be made for so much of the consideration as is normally attributable to it.
(5) For the purposes of this Law, the normal value of the delivery of goods or provision of services is taken to be the amount that would be considered as its value by virtue of subsection (2) above if the transaction was carried out for as much monetary consideration as would be payable by a person who does not have such a relationship with any person that would affect that consideration.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 38(I)/2008)*

## Value of acquisitions from other Member States
14A.-(1) Subject to article 13C and for the purposes of this Law, the value of any acquisition of goods from another member state is taken to be the value of the act by which they are acquired.
(1A) For the purposes of this Law, the value of goods acquired from another member state is taken to include the following:
(a) consumption taxes, excise duties, import duties imposed either outside the Republic, or due to the acquisition within the Republic, excluding VAT; and
(b) all incidental costs, such as supply, packaging, transport and insurance, borne by the supplier to the buyer or recipient, up to the first destination of the goods in the Republic; and
for the purposes of this subsection, "first destination" means the place of first unloading of the goods in the Republic.
(2) When goods are acquired from another member state not in the execution of a taxable transaction, the value of the act in execution of which they are acquired, is determined for the purposes of subsection (1) in accordance with this article and Part II of the Fourth Schedule, and for these purposes-
(a) subsections (3) to (5) apply subject to those provisions of the Fourth Schedule; and
(b) section 14 and Part I of the Fourth Schedule do not apply in relation to the deed.
(3) If the act is carried out against monetary consideration, its value is taken to be an amount equal to the consideration.
(4) If the act is carried out against non-monetary consideration or not entirely monetary consideration, its value is taken to be as much monetary amount as is equal to the consideration.
(5) Where an act in execution of which goods are acquired from another Member State is not the only thing to which a pecuniary consideration relates, the act shall be deemed to be carried out for so much of the consideration as is normally attributable to it.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Value of imported goods
15.—(1) For the purposes of this Act, the value of goods imported from a place outside the Member States shall be determined (subject to the provisions of subsection (2) below and section (16) in accordance with the rules applicable in the case of import duties, whether or not the goods in question are subject to any such duties.
(2) For the purposes of this Act the value of goods imported from a place outside the Member States shall be taken to include the following to the extent not already included in that value in accordance with the rules referred to in subsection (1) above, namely—
(a) All taxes, excise duties, excise and import duties and other charges imposed either outside the Republic or, by reason of the importation, within the Republic (excluding VAT); and
(b) all incidental costs such as supply, packaging, transport and insurance, up to the first destination of the goods in the Republic.
(c) if at the time of the importation of the goods from a place outside the Member States the further destination of the goods is known, and that destination is within the Republic or another Member State, all incidental costs to the extent resulting from the transport of the goods to that other destination;
and in this subsection "first destination of goods" means the place stated in the consignment note or any other document pursuant to which the goods are imported into the Republic, or in the absence of such document, means the place of first unloading of the goods in the Republic.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Value of goods delivered before customs clearance
16.—(1) Without prejudice to subsection (2) of article 15, when goods imported into the Republic are delivered, prior to their customs clearance for internal consumption, their value for the purposes of imposing VAT. upon their import is determined according to the value of the delivery to the person who submits the relevant declaration to customs.
(2) The value of the delivery taken into account pursuant to subsection (1) of this section may not be less than the value determined pursuant to subsection (1) of section 15.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Normal factor
17. Subject to the provisions of article 18 and any decrees issued pursuant thereto, VAT is imposed at a rate of eighteen percent (18%) for the period from 14 January 2013 to 12 January 2014 and at a rate of nineteen percent (19%) from 13 January 2014 and is imposed -
(a) On the delivery of goods or provision of services, with reference to the value of the transaction as determined pursuant to this Law; and
(aa) on the acquisition of goods from another member state, with reference to the value of the acquisition as determined pursuant to this Law; and;
(b) on the importation of goods from a place outside the Member States, with reference to the value of the goods as determined under this Law.

(b) of any corresponding acquisition or importation, shall be levied at the rate of five percent (5%).

18B.-(1) The F.P.A. imposed on
(a) of any transaction falling within the Fifteenth Schedule from time to time. or

at a rate of three percent (3%).

## Input tax and output tax
19.—(1) Subject to the following provisions of this section, "input tax", in relation to a taxable person, means:
(a) VAT on the delivery to it of any goods or the provision of any services to it;
(b) the VAT on its acquisition of any goods from another member state; and
(c) VAT paid or payable by that person on the importation from a place outside the Member States of any goods which are (in any case) goods or services used or to be used for the purposes of any business carried on or to be carried on by that person.
(2) Subject to the following provisions of this article, "output tax", in relation to a taxable person, means VAT. on the transactions carried out by the person in question on the acquisition of goods from another member state, including VAT. which is also calculated as input tax under sub-section (1)(b).
(3) [Deleted]
(4) Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may provide in relation to any description of goods or services that, when goods or services of that description are delivered or provided to a person who is not a taxable person, they shall be deemed, under such circumstances as may be specified in the Regulations, for the purposes of subsections (1) and (2) above, to be delivered or provided to some other person as may be specified in the Regulations.
(5) When goods delivered or services rendered to a taxable person, goods acquired by a taxable person from another Member State or goods imported by a taxable person from a place outside the Member States, are used or are to be used partly for the purposes of a business carried on or to be carried on by that person and partly for other purposes -
(a) VAT on transactions, acquisitions and imports is apportioned so that only so much VAT that which relates to his business purposes to be calculated as his input tax;
(b) the remaining VAT, which does not relate to its business purposes, will be calculated as its input tax, to the extent provided by regulations issued pursuant to subsection 6(e).
(5A) For the purposes of subsections (1) and (5), relevant property for the purposes of a business carried on or to be carried on by that person shall not, under any circumstances, be deemed to be used or to be used for the purposes of the business if, and to the extent it is used for private use by that person or for private use by his staff.
(5B) For the purposes of subsection 5A “relevant asset” means—
(a)(i) the tangible goods, which belong by ownership to the enterprise and are placed by it in continuous exploitation, without including in their value any costs of repair and maintenance thereof, excluding immovable property;
(ii) the rights to use patents, designs, models, trade or industrial marks and similar rights, including goodwill, as long as these serve more than one use, delivered or provided to or imported or acquired by the owner and the value of each is not less than seventeen thousand euros (€17000);
(b)(i) immovable property as specified in paragraph 1(b) to 4 of the Eighth Schedule to the Act;
(ii) buildings constructed by a taxable person on immovable property not owned by him;
(iii) buildings constructed by a taxable person on immovable property owned by him.
(6) Regulations may provide that—
(a) VAT on the delivery of goods or provision of services to a taxable person, the VAT on the acquisition of goods by a taxable person from another member state and the VAT paid or payable by a taxable person on the importation of goods from a place outside the Member States is considered as input tax only if to the extent that the imposition of VAT. is proved and quantified by reference to such documents as may be specified in the Regulations or as the Commissioner may specify either generally or in specific cases or classes of cases;
(b) a taxable person calculates as his input tax, under such circumstances, to such extent and subject to such conditions as may be determined, the VAT. on the supply of goods or provision of services to it or on the acquisition of goods by it from another Member State or paid by it on the importation of goods from a place outside the Member States even if it was not a taxable person at the time of the transaction, acquisition or payment;
(c) a taxable person who is a legal entity calculates as his input tax, under such circumstances, to such extent and subject to such conditions as may be determined, the VAT. on the delivery, acquisition or importation of goods effected before its incorporation for the purpose of being attributed to it or its business or on the rendering of services before that time for its benefit or in connection with its incorporation;
(d) in the case of a person who was, but is no longer, a taxable person, the amount of any VAT is paid to him by the Commissioner. on the provision of services carried out to him for the purposes of the business that this person carried out when he was a taxable person.
(e) in the cases where the settlement of the input tax is carried out pursuant to the provisions of subsection (5), the part of the VAT. which does not relate to business purposes, to be considered as input tax of the taxable person for the purposes of any provisions under or based on article 21, in such circumstances, to such extent and under such conditions as may be determined.
(7) [Deleted]

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 37(I)/2011)*

## Payment with reference to tax periods and deduction of input tax against output tax
20.—(1) Every taxable person shall render an account and pay VAT, in respect of transactions carried on by him and in respect of acquisition by such person of any goods from other Member States, in respect of such periods (referred to in this Act as "specified tax periods") at such time and in such manner as may be prescribed by or under Regulations and such Regulations may otherwise provide for different circumstances.
(2) Subject to the provisions of this section, every taxable person is entitled at the end of each specified tax period to deduct such amount of his input tax as is permitted under section 21, and then to deduct such amount from any output tax due from him.
(3) If there is no output tax due at the end of the specified tax period, or if the amount deducted exceeds that of the output tax then, subject to subsections (4) and (5) below, the amount of the deduction or, as the case may be, the amount of the difference shall be paid to the taxable person by the Commissioner; and an amount due under this subsection is referred to in this Act as a "credit balance VAT.".
(4) The whole or part of the credit balance may, in accordance with general or special instructions issued by the Commissioner from time to time be transferred to the credit of the said person at a later period.
It is provided that, in the event that a claim has been submitted pursuant to subsection (6) and the payment of all or part of the credit balance for which the claim was submitted, has been transferred to the credit of the taxable person in a later period or in the event that for any reason it is not due to the fault or act or omission of this person, the return thereof is delayed for a period of time beyond four (4) months from the date of submission of the claim, the refundable amount bears interest from the day the four (4) month period expires, in accordance with the Uniform Public Default Interest Law:
It is further provided that in the event that a tax audit is conducted by the Registrar in relation to the claim, the four (4) month time period shall be extended to eight (8) months.
(5) When at the end of any period a VAT credit balance is due to a taxable person who has failed to file returns for any previous period as required by this Law, the Commissioner may suspend payment of the credit balance until such person complies with the said obligation.
(5A) When at the end of any period a VAT credit balance is owed to a taxable person who, by the date the claim for refund is submitted, has failed to submit an income declaration in accordance with the provisions of article 5 of the Tax Certification and Collection Law, the Commissioner may suspend the payment of the credit balance until the person in question complies with his obligation:
It is provided that, regardless of the provisions of this Law, no interest is due from the Registrar regarding the period in which the VAT refund. suspended due to failure to submit any income statement pursuant to Article 5 of the Tax Certification and Collection Law.
(6) Deduction pursuant to subsection (2) above and payment of VAT credit balance. is made only by a demand submitted in such manner and at such time as may be determined by or based on Regulations; and, in the case of a person who has not carried out taxable transactions during the period in question or in a previous period, the payment of the VAT credit balance is made subject to such conditions (if any) as the Commissioner considers appropriate to impose, including conditions for refund in specified circumstances.
(6A) No claim for refund of VAT credit balance. may be submitted after the lapse of six (6) years from the end of the specified tax period in which the credit balance was created:
Provided that, the Registrar may allow the submission of such a claim under circumstances which he deems reasonable.
(7) Regulations made by the Council of Ministers may provide that, in relation to certain transactions, acquisitions and imports which the Regulations may specify, the VAT charged thereon shall be exempt from any deduction under this section; and any such provision may be made by reference to the description of the goods supplied or the services rendered, the goods acquired or the goods imported, to the person by whom they are supplied or supplied, acquired or imported, for the purposes for which they are delivered or supplied, acquired or imported, or in any circumstances and such Regulations may provide for consequential exemption from output tax.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 135(I)/2012
 
 
 
 
 122(I)/2020)*

## VAT on excise goods or new means of transport
20A. Regardless of any other provisions of this law, the VAT on any goods subject to excise duty or new means of transport subject to excise duty, shall be levied and paid as if they were excise duty or excise duty respectively:
Provided that, in case of acquisition, the above provision applies if the acquisition of the goods is carried out by a taxable person.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Input tax allowed under Article 20
21.—(1) The amount of input tax which a taxable person is entitled to deduct at the end of any period is such amount of his input tax for the period (that is, input tax on transactions, acquisitions and imports made by that person during the period) as is allowed by or under the Regulations as attributable to transactions falling within subsection (2) below.
(2) The transactions referred to in subsection (1) are the following transactions carried out or to be carried out by a taxable person within the framework of or for the promotion of his business:
(a) Taxable Transactions;
(b) transactions outside the Republic that would be taxable transactions if carried out within the Republic.
(c) transactions falling within paragraph 1 and sub-paragraphs (a) to (e) of paragraph 3 of Table B of the Seventh Schedule if the recipient is established outside the Member States or such services are directly related to goods exported to a country outside the Member States.
(2A) Supplies deemed to be made by the recipient in accordance with section 11A shall be taken into account as supplies made by him for the purposes of calculating any amount of VAT. to which he is entitled under subsection (1), only during the time that the deliveries in question are deemed to be made by virtue of regulations issued on the basis of article 9.
(3) Regulations made by the Cabinet may provide for the securing of a fair and reasonable return of input tax on transactions falling within subsection (2) above, and such Regulations may provide—
(a) Fixing a ratio by reference to which input tax for any specified tax period is provisionally attributed to those transactions;
(b) readjusting the tax provisionally assessed for any specified tax periods or parts thereof, in accordance with a proportion similarly determined for any longer period comprising those periods or parts thereof;
(c) the making of payments in respect of input tax, by the Commissioner to a taxable person (or to a person who was a taxable person) or by a taxable person (or a person who was a taxable person) to the Commissioner, in cases where the facts prove that the calculation on the basis of which the return was made was incorrect; and
(d) exclusion of input tax on a transaction which, by virtue of any provision of this Act, a person carries out to himself from being allowable because it is attributed to that transaction.
(4) Regulations under subsection (3) above may provide differently for different circumstances and, in particular (but without affecting the generality of that subsection) for different descriptions of goods or services; and may contain such incidental and supplementary provisions as the Cabinet deems necessary or expedient.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 88(I)/2005
 
 
 
 
 141(I)/2007)*

## Goods imported for private purposes
22.—(1) When goods are imported by a taxable person from a place outside the Member States and—
(a) At the time of introduction they are fully or partially owned by another person; and
(b) the purposes for which they are to be used include private purposes of either the same or the other, the VAT paid or payable by the taxable person on the importation of the goods shall not be considered as input tax deducted or deducted pursuant to Article 20; but this person may submit a separate claim to the Registrar to refund it to him.
(2) The Commissioner approves the requirement referred to in subsection (1) if he is satisfied that its rejection would result in a double imposition of VAT. and when it approves it, it shall approve it only to the extent necessary to avoid double imposition.
(3) In examining a claim under this section, the Commissioner shall take into account the circumstances of the importation and, to the extent he considers relevant, events taking place or occurring in relation to the goods at any subsequent time.
(4) Any amount of the claim approved by the Commissioner shall be paid to the taxable person.
(5) The reference above to private purposes of a person refers to purposes other than those of the business carried on by that person.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Advance payment of VAT
23.—(1) The Council of Ministers may, if it deems appropriate, issue a decree under this article in the interest of the national economy.
(2) An order under this section may provide that certain classes of taxable persons specified in the order shall have a duty—
(a) To pay, against any amount of VAT who may become liable to pay in respect of a specified tax period, amounts specified in accordance with the Ordinance, and
(b) to pay them at times specified in the decree.
(3) When an order is issued under this section, the Commissioner may issue notices containing any additional, incidental or consequential provisions that he deems necessary or appropriate.
(4) An order or notification under this section may provide otherwise for different circumstances.

*(95(I)/2000)*

## Invoices issued by recipients of goods or services
24. When—
(a) A taxable person ("the recipient") issues a document to himself that is an invoice item in respect of a taxable supply of goods or services to him by another taxable person ("the supplier"); and
(b) less VAT is declared in said document. from that which is enforceable in the transaction,
the Registrar may, with a notification served on the recipient and the supplier, determine that the amount of VAT declared less in the document is considered for all purposes as VAT. due by the recipient and not by the supplier.

*(95(I)/2000)*

## Zero coefficient
25.—(1) When a taxable person delivers goods or renders services and the transaction is subject to the zero rate then, regardless of whether VAT would be chargeable. on the transaction notwithstanding this subsection or not—
(a) No VAT is imposed. on the transaction; but
(b) in all other respects it is considered as a taxable transaction; and accordingly the rate at which VAT is considered to be imposed. on the transaction is zero.
(2) A supply of goods or services is zero-rated under this subsection if the goods or services or the transaction are described in the Sixth Schedule.
(3) When goods described in the Sixth Annex, or goods that are part of a transaction described in the Sixth Annex, are acquired in the Republic from another member state or imported from a place outside the member states, no VAT is imposed. upon their acquisition or importation, unless otherwise provided in said Annex.
(4) The Council of Ministers may, by decree published in the official Gazette of the Republic, amend the Sixth Annex by adding or removing from it any description or by amending any description specified in it from time to time.
(5) Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers may provide for the inclusion of the zero rate or the refund of all or part of the VAT. of the person liable for the delivery of goods or the provision of services to certain bodies or persons, subject to such conditions (including conditions that prohibit or limit the disposal or trading of the goods) that may be imposed based on the Regulations, if and to the extent that the Council of Ministers determines that the inclusion in the zero rate or the refund is necessary or appropriate, taking into account any international agreement.
(6) A supply of goods is subject to the nil rate under this subsection if the Commissioner is satisfied that the person delivering the goods—
(a) He has exported them to a place outside the member states; or
(b) has loaded them for use as supplies for a sea route or air flight with a final destination outside the Republic or as goods for retail sale to persons transported on the said route or flight on a ship or plane with a destination outside the member states, and provided that any other conditions that may be determined in Regulations or imposed by the Commissioner are met.
(7) Subsection 6(b) above does not apply in the case of goods loaded for use as supplies on a sea route or air flight undertaken by the person to whom the goods are delivered and the said route or flight is undertaken for private purposes.
(8) Regulations may provide for the imposition of a zero rate on supplies of certain goods as may be specified in the Regulations, in cases where—
(a) The Commissioner is satisfied that the goods have been, or are to be, exported to a place outside the Member States or that such delivery involves -
(i) the transport of the goods from the Republic; and
(ii) the acquisition of the goods in another member state by a person who is liable for the payment of VAT. on the acquisition in accordance with the corresponding provisions of Article 12A of the law of that Member State; and
(b) such other conditions, if any, as may be specified in the Regulations or imposed by the Commissioner are satisfied.
(8A) Regulations may provide for the imposition of a zero rate on deliveries of goods or certain goods as may be specified in the Regulations in cases where -
(a) the Commissioner is satisfied that the said delivery entails—
(i) the movement of goods from a VAT storage regime. which falls within the meaning of section 13F(2) and
(ii) placing them under a storage regime in another Member State, or in such Member State or Member States as may be specified, where that regime has been established by virtue of provisions corresponding to Articles 13A and 13B of the law of that Member State; and
(b) such other conditions, if any, as may be specified in the Regulations or imposed by the Registrar are satisfied.
(9) Regulations may provide for the levying of a zero rate on a supply of services which takes place where goods are leased and the Commissioner is satisfied that the goods have been or are to be transferred from the Republic during the period of the lease, and provided that such other conditions are satisfied, if any, as may be specified in the Regulations or imposed by the Commissioner.
(10) Where the supply of any goods subject to zero-rating under subsection (6) above or under Regulations made under subsection (8), (8A) or (9) above and—
(a) The goods are in the Republic after the date on which they were alleged to have been exported or loaded or to be exported or loaded or otherwise transported or to be transported from the Republic; or
(b) any condition specified in the relevant Regulations issued under subsections (6), (8), (8A) or (9) above or imposed by the Commissioner is not satisfied, and the presence of the goods in the Republic after the said date or the non-fulfillment of the condition had not been approved for the purposes of this subsection by the Commissioner, the goods are subject to confiscation under the Customs and Excise Act Consumer Law and VAT which would be chargeable upon delivery if the zero rate did not exist, becomes immediately payable by the person to whom the goods are delivered or by any person in whose possession they are in the Republic; but the Commissioner may, if he deems it appropriate, not to require the payment of all or part of the amount of VAT.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Excluded transactions and acquisitions
26.—(1) The supply of goods or the provision of services is an exempt transaction if described in the Seventh Schedule, the acquisition of goods from another Member State is an exempt acquisition if the goods were acquired pursuant to an exempt transaction and the import of goods is an exempt import if the delivery of such goods within the Republic is in all cases exempt.
(2) The Council of Ministers may, by decree published in the Official Gazette of the Republic, amend the Seventh Schedule by adding or deleting from it any description of a transaction or by modifying any description of a transaction specified in it from time to time, and the Schedule may be amended so as to describe the delivery of goods with reference to their use or to other matters not related to the characteristics of the goods themselves.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Bad debt
27.—(1) Subsection (2) below applies where—
(a) A person has delivered goods or provided services and has rendered and paid VAT. on the transaction,
(b) all or any part of the consideration for the transaction has been written off in its books as a bad debt;
(c) has taken the measures necessary under the circumstances for its collection, and
(d) a period of twelve months (beginning on the date of the transaction) has elapsed;
(2) Subject to the following provisions of this article and Regulations issued pursuant to it, the person referred to in subsection (1) is entitled, after submitting a claim to the Registrar, to a refund of the amount of VAT. which is enforceable with reference to the amount due.
(3) In subsection (2) above “amount due” means—
(a) If at the time of the demand the person has not received payment as part of the consideration written off in his books as a bad debt, an amount equal to the amount of the consideration written off—
(b) if at the time of the claim he has received a payment or payments as part of the consideration written off, an amount by which the amount of consideration written off exceeds the payment (or the sum of the payments).
(4) No person shall be entitled to a refund under subsection (2) above unless the value of the transaction is equal to, or. less than its normal value.
(5) When—
(a) A person is entitled by virtue of subsection (2) above to be refunded an amount of VAT, and
(b) that VAT has at any time been included in the input tax of another person, that other person is considered, from the time the claim for tax refund is submitted, not to be entitled to any input tax deduction in relation to VAT. which must be returned according to that claim.
(6) Regulations under this section may—
(a) To require that a demand be submitted at such time and in such form and manner as may be specified in the said Regulations-
(b) require that the claim be proven and quantified with reference to records and other documents that may be determined by said Regulations-
(c) require the applicant to maintain, for such period and in such form and manner as may be specified in the Regulations, such records and documents and a record of information relating to the claim and subsequent payments as part of the consideration-
(d) to demand the repayment of the money allowed to be returned under this article when there is no compliance with any provision of the Regulations-
(e) require the repayment of the whole or, as the case may be, a proportionate part of the money allowed to be refunded under this section where the applicant receives a subsequent payment (or further payment) as part of the consideration written off from his books as a bad debt;
(f) include supplementary, incidental, incidental or transitional provisions that the Council of Ministers deems necessary or appropriate for the purposes of this article;
(g) provide differently for different situations.
(7) The provisions that can be included in Regulations pursuant to the subsection
(6)(f) above may include rules for ascertaining—
