 80(I)/2021)*

## Tax offenses
46.—(1) Any person involved in fraudulent evasion of VAT payment. or takes actions with the purpose of fraudulently avoiding the payment of VAT, either by himself or by any other person, is guilty of a criminal offense and is subject to a fine up to three times the amount of VAT. which is due or to imprisonment for up to three years or to both of these penalties.
(2) Any reference to subsection (1) above or subsection (6) below to avoid payment of VAT. includes reference to collateral—
(a) Payment of VAT credit balance; or
(b) refund of tax under section 27 30A or 42 of this Law or section 31 of the 1990 Law; or
(c) refund of tax in accordance with regulations issued pursuant to article 12D(5); or
(d) tax refund pursuant to article 30, and any reference of the aforementioned sections to the amount of VAT. interpreted—
(i) in relation to the VAT itself. or with a VAT credit balance, as a reference to the sum of the amount (if any) falsely claimed with input tax credit and the amount (if any) in which less output tax was falsely declared, and
(ii) in relation to a tax refund falling within paragraphs (b) (c) or (d) above, as a reference to the amount falsely claimed as a tax refund.
(3) Any person—
(a) With intent to defraud, produces, delivers or sends for the purposes of this Act or otherwise uses for those purposes any document which is materially untrue; or
(b) in providing any information for the purposes of this Act makes any statement which he knows to be materially untrue or recklessly makes a materially untrue statement,
is guilty of a criminal offense and is subject to a fine up to three times the amount of VAT. which is due or to imprisonment for up to three years or to both of these penalties.
(4) The reference in subsection 3 (a) above to bringing, delivering, sending or otherwise using a document which is materially untrue, with intent to deceive, includes a reference to delivering, sending or otherwise using such a document, with intent to cause a device to respond to the document as if it were a true document.
(5) The reference in subsections (3)(a) and (4) above to bringing, delivering or sending a document includes reference to any act that results in a document being brought, delivered or sent.
(5A) Without prejudice to the provisions of subsections (3) and (5), any person who for the purposes of this Law:
(a) imports, delivers or sends or otherwise uses any document which is materially untrue or,
(b) in providing information makes any statement which is materially untrue;
is guilty of a criminal offense and liable to a fine of up to one thousand pounds.
(6) When the behavior of a person during any specific period of time entails the commission of one or more offenses by virtue of the previous provisions of this article, then, regardless of whether the elements of the offense or offenses are known or not, he is guilty of a criminal offense and is subject to a fine up to three times the amount of VAT. which is due or to imprisonment for up to three years or to both of these penalties.
(7) Any person acquires possession of any goods or trades in any goods, or accepts the provision of any services, having reason to believe that the payment of VAT. of the obligee on the delivery of the goods or the provision of the services, on the acquisition of the goods from another Member State or on the importation of the goods from a place outside the Member States has or is to be avoided, is guilty of a criminal offense and liable to a fine of up to five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for up to twelve months or to both of these penalties.
(8) Any person who delivers goods or renders services in contravention of a condition imposed under paragraph 3(2) of the Tenth Schedule shall be guilty of an offense and liable to a fine not exceeding five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding twelve months or to both.
(9) Any person who fails to pay VAT. who appears in his tax return as being payable in respect of any period within the period prescribed by Regulations issued under section 20(1), is guilty of a criminal offense and is liable to a fine of up to five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for a term of up to twelve months, or to both.
(10) Any person who fails to submit a tax return for a specified tax period within the period prescribed by Regulations issued under section 20(1), is guilty of a criminal offense and liable to a fine of up to five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for a term of up to twelve months or to both.
(10A) Any person who omits or refuses to pay to the Commissioner any amount of additional tax or pecuniary charge or interest imposed under the provisions of this Law or the Regulations issued thereunder shall be guilty of a criminal offense and liable to a fine of up to ten percent (10%) of the amount due.
(11) Any person who omits or refuses to pay any amount of VAT to the Registrar. ascertained in accordance with the provisions of articles 49 and 49A, within thirty days of receiving the relevant notification, is guilty of a criminal offense and is liable to a fine of up to five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for a term of up to twelve months or to both of these penalties.
(11A) Any person who fails to issue a legal receipt and to deliver a legal receipt at the time of the transaction in accordance with regulations made under paragraph 1A(1) of the Tenth Schedule is guilty of an offense and on conviction is liable to a fine not exceeding one thousand seven hundred euros (€1700) or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding three (3) years, or to both. penalties.
(11B) Any person who fails to submit to the Commissioner a Recapitulation Table under section 42C(2) is guilty of a criminal offense and is liable to a fine of up to eight hundred and fifty euros (€850).
(11C) A person who fails to comply with the provisions of the regulations issued under paragraph 5A of the Tenth Schedule is guilty of an offense and, on conviction, is liable to a fine not exceeding twenty thousand euros (€20,000) or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding twelve (12) months, or to both.
(12) Notwithstanding the provisions of any other law, a criminal court finding any person guilty of failure to pay to the Commissioner any amount due under the provisions of this Act, including money charges and interest, shall have power, in addition to imposing a penalty, to make an order ordering the person convicted to pay the said amount to the Commissioner.
(13) Notwithstanding the provisions of any other law, an order made under subsection (12) shall be deemed to be a judgment of a civil court and may be made, signed and enforced as a judgment in an action in accordance with the provisions of the Civil Procedure Act.
(14) Sections 87, 88 and 121 of the Customs Code Laws of 2004 apply mutatis mutandis in relation to criminal offenses provided for in this Law and any reference in those sections to duty or tax shall be construed as a reference to VAT, any reference to the Director shall be construed as a reference to the Commissioner of VAT. and the reference of article 88 to the provisions of article 89(3), (4) and (5) of the Customs Code Laws of 2004, is interpreted as a reference to article 47 of this Law.
(15) Subject to the provisions of the preceding subsections of this article, any person who refuses or omits or neglects to comply with the obligations imposed by the provisions of the Tenth Schedule or any regulations or decrees issued on the basis of this Schedule, is guilty of a criminal offense and liable to a fine of up to five thousand pounds (L.C. 5,000) or to imprisonment for up to twelve (12) months or both of these penalties.
(16) A person who submitted an application, with false information, for the imposition of a reduced rate for the acquisition of a residence in accordance with the provisions of Table C, of the Fifth Schedule, and paid tax based on the reduced rate is required to pay the difference between the amounts of tax resulting from the application of the two rates, reduced and normal, on the value of the residence and is additionally guilty of a criminal offense and subject to sentencing him to a fine not exceeding twice the amount he is required to pay under this subsection or to imprisonment for up to three years or to both of these penalties.

## Special provisions for tax declarations for which the VAT paid by December 10, 2013
46(A). - (1) The provisions of subsections (3) and (4) of article 45 and subsections (9) and (10A) of article 46 do not apply for the specified tax period ending on February 28, 2013, March 31, 2013, April 30, 2013 and May 31, 2013, provided that the taxable person submits the tax return for these periods and pay the amount of VAT due. until December 10, 2013.
(2) The provisions of subsections (3) and (4) of article 45 and subsections (9) and (10A) of article 46 do not apply for a specified tax period ending on 29h February 2020, on 31h March 2020 and the 30or April 2020, provided that the taxable person pays the amount of VAT due in six (6) equal installments, each payable no later than the tenth of each calendar month, in the period starting from the month of November 2020 and ending on 10h April 2021:
Provided that the provisions of subsection (2) do not apply to taxable persons with economic activity codes belonging to the following categories: 35111 (Electricity producers), 36001 (Collection and distribution of water (for water supply), 47111 (grocery stores and supermarkets mainly for food), 47112 (kiosks-mini markets), 47191 (Retail sale of a variety of goods in department stores where food, beverages and tobacco do not predominate), 47211 (Retail sale of fruits and vegetables - fruit stores), 47221 (Retail sale of meat and meat products including poultry), 47231 (Retail sale of fish and seafood - fish, molluscs), 47241 (Retail sale of meat and meat products including poultry) bread and other bakery products), 47242 (Retail sale of confectionery), 47301 (Retail sale of fuel), 47411 (Retail sale of computers, peripheral equipment and software incl. video games), 47611 (Retail sale of books), 47621 (Retail sale of newspapers and stationery), 47651 (Retail trade of games of all kinds except video games), 47731 (Pharmacies), 61101 (Telecommunications Authority of Cyprus), 61201 (Internet services), 61301 (Satellite telecommunications services), 61901 (other telecommunications services except ATHIK).

## Offenses concerning the Superintendent, officials, etc
47.—(1) Whoever, in order to achieve entry into any house or place or to achieve or cause the performance of any act, without having such a right, or for any other illegal purpose duplicates the name, designation or capacity of the Commissioner, an officer of the VAT Service. or other authorized person, is guilty of a criminal offense and is liable, in addition to any other criminal liability to which he may be liable, to a fine of up to five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for a term of up to two years or to both.
(2) Whoever, having an order or other written authorization from the Superintendent, is called upon by the Superintendent to return it or to provide satisfactory explanations for the said order or authorization and fails to do so within the time limit set by the Superintendent, commits a criminal offense and is liable to a fine of up to one hundred pounds; and if the failure continues after his conviction, he is guilty of continuation of the commission of a criminal offense and is liable to a fine of up to ten pounds for each day that the omission continues.
(3) If the Commissioner, an officer of the VAT Service or other authorized person—
(a) Directly or indirectly requests or receives, in connection with any of his duties, payment or other remuneration of any kind, monetary or otherwise, or any promise or assurance of such payment or remuneration, which he may not by law demand or receive; or
(b) conclude or consent to the conclusion of an agreement, do something or omit to do, permit, conceal or tolerate an act concerning the implementation of this Law and which induces persecution of the Republic or which is otherwise illegal,
is guilty of a criminal offense and is liable to a fine not exceeding five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding three years or to both.
(4) Whoever—
(a) Directly or indirectly offers or provides the Commissioner, an officer of the VAT Service. or other authorized person any payment or other consideration of any kind, pecuniary or otherwise, or any promise or assurance of such payment or consideration; or
(b) proposes or enters into an agreement with the Commissioner, an officer of the VAT Service. or other authorized person, in order to persuade him to do or refrain from doing any act, to allow, cover up or tolerate any act, which concerns the implementation of this Law and which induces harassment of the Republic or which is otherwise illegal or otherwise to do something in defiance of his duties, is guilty of a criminal offense and is subject to a fine up to five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for up to three years or to both of these penalties.
(5) Whoever—
(a) Obstructs, obstructs, harasses or unlawfully attacks a person who is duly engaged in the exercise of any duty or power conferred upon him by virtue of a legislative provision relating to the application of this Law or against a person who seeks his assistance; or
(b) does anything which obstructs or is intended to obstruct the receipt of records and documents, the carrying out of an inspection to find goods subject to confiscation or the carrying out of an investigation to find evidence of the commission of an offence, or the prevention of the detention, seizure or transfer of such records, documents, goods or evidence by the Commissioner, an officer of the Service VAT or other authorized person; or
(c) seizes, damages or destroys any such records, documents, goods or evidence, or does anything intended to prevent the leading of evidence or testimony as to the lawfulness or otherwise of such receipt, inspection, search or seizure; or
(d) obstructs the detention of any person by a person duly acting as aforesaid or releases a person duly detained, or who attempts to do any of the above, is guilty of an offense and liable to a fine not exceeding five thousand pounds or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding three years or to both.
(6) Whoever, without reasonable cause, refuses to appear for examination as required by subsection (6) of paragraph 9 of the Tenth Schedule, is guilty of an offense and liable to a fine which may extend to one thousand pounds or to imprisonment for a term which may extend to six months, or to both.

*(95(I)/2000)*

## Criminal liability of consultants, etc
48.—(1) When a criminal offense referred to in this Law is committed by a legal entity, the responsibility for that offense shall be borne by the legal entity itself, the advisers or the managing officers of the legal entity.
(2) For the purposes of this section "executive officer", in relation to a body corporate, means any director, secretary or other similar officer of the body corporate or any person purporting to be acting in any such capacity or as a consultant.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Failure to submit tax returns, etc
49.—(1) Where any person fails to file any tax returns required under this Act (or under any provision repealed by this Act) or to keep any documents and give facilities necessary to verify such returns or where the Commissioner considers that such returns are incomplete or inaccurate, the Commissioner may in his best judgment ascertain the amount of VAT due from that person and to notify the amount to that person.
(2) In any case where, for any specified taxable period, there has been paid or credited to any person—
(a) As a VAT refund, or
(b) as a VAT credit balance, due to it, any amount which should not have been paid or credited, or which should not have been paid or credited if the facts were known or if they were as they are subsequently shown to be, the Commissioner may certify this amount as VAT. due from the person for that period and notify the amount to that person.
(3) An amount which—
(a) It had been paid to any person as due to him as a VAT credit balance, and
(b) by reason of the cancellation of that person's registration under paragraph 13(2) to (4) of Part I, paragraph 6(2) of Part II or paragraph 6(2) or (3) of Part III of the First Schedule, should not have been paid to him, may be ascertained under subsection (2) above, regardless of the fact of the cancellation.
(4) When VAT is confirmed. by virtue of subsections (1) and (2) above in relation to the same specified tax period, the certificates may be combined and communicated to the person to whom they relate as one certificate.
(5) When a person who fails to submit a tax return, or who submits a tax return which the Commissioner deems to be incomplete or inaccurate, was required to submit the tax return as a personal representative, bankruptcy trustee, receiver, administrator, liquidator or person otherwise acting in a representative capacity in relation to another person, then subsection (1) above shall apply as if the reference to VAT. the amount owed by this person to include a reference to VAT. the one owed by the other person.
(6) When a taxable person, in the context of or for the promotion of a business that carries on—
(a) Has acquired any goods delivered to it, has acquired any goods from another Member State or has otherwise acquired possession or control of any goods, or
(b) has imported any goods from a place outside the Member States;
then the Commissioner may demand from such person as he may from time to time give an account of the goods; and if he fails to prove that the goods have been delivered or are available to be delivered, or that they have been exported or otherwise conveyed from the Republic without being exported or so conveyed in the form of delivery, or that they have been lost or destroyed, the Commissioner may in his best judgment certify and notify the person the amount of VAT which would have been enforceable in relation to the delivery of the goods if they had been delivered by it.
(6A) When a tax depositor fails to pay the VAT. required by the Commissioner pursuant to article 13E(2), the Commissioner may, at his best discretion, certify the amount of VAT. due from that person and to notify the amount to that person.
(6B) When the Commissioner considers that goods have been moved from a warehouse or tax warehouse without payment of the VAT payable. pursuant to article 13D(4) on this movement, the Commissioner may, at his best discretion, certify the amount of VAT. which is due from the person who moved the goods or another person liable and to notify the amount to that person.
(7) In any case where—
(a) As a result of a person's failure to file a tax return for a specified tax period, the Commissioner has issued a certificate under subsection (1) above for that period,
(b) the VAT certified has been paid but no regular tax return has been filed for the period covered by the certification, and
(c) as a result of a failure to file a tax return for a later specified tax period, which is a failure by a person referred to in paragraph (a) above or a person acting in a representative capacity in relation to it, as referred to in subsection (5) above, the Commissioner considers it necessary to issue another certificate under subsection (1) above, then, if the Commissioner considers it appropriate, having regard to the failure referred to in paragraph (a) above, may specify in the certificate referred to in paragraph (c) above a VAT amount. greater than he would otherwise consider appropriate.
(7A) Where any person, as specified in section 45(7) or in regulations made under section 42(12), issues an invoice or receipt showing an amount to be VAT. or that it includes an amount attributable to VAT, then the Registrar may certify the amount shown as VAT. or, as the case may be, taken to represent VAT, as VAT. due from the person issuing or, as the case may be, receiving the invoice or receipt and to notify the amount to that person:
Provided that, in the event that the amount of VAT includes VAT on the acquisition of goods from another member state and the Commissioner is not satisfied that the VAT amount in question has been paid, by virtue of the law of another Member State on the delivery as a result of which those goods were acquired, then the Commissioner may certify the difference.
(7B) When any person secures without being entitled to an amount:
(a) Refund of tax under regulations made under section 12D(5).
(b) a refund of tax under section 27 or 30A of this Act or section 31 of the 1990 Act; and
(c) refund of tax pursuant to article 30,
then the Registrar may certify this amount as VAT. due from the person and notify the amount to that person.
(7C) When any person pursuant to article 45(7A), delivers or prepares an inaccurate certificate, the Registrar may certify -
(a) in the case of subsection (7A)(a), the difference between –
(i) the amount of VAT which would have been enforceable on the transaction or transactions if the certificate had been accurate; and
(ii) the amount of VAT which indeed is imposing.
(b) in the case of subsection (7A)(b), the amount of VAT which is actually enforceable upon acquisition,
as VAT due from the person who, as the case may be, or prepares the certificate and to notify the amount to that person.
(8) When any amount has been ascertained and notified to any person pursuant to subsections (1), (2), (3), (6), (6A), (6B), (7A), (7B) or (7C) above, then this is considered, subject to the provisions of this Law on objections, as a VAT amount. due from such person and may be recovered accordingly, unless and to the extent that the certificate has been subsequently revoked or amended by reducing the amount.
(9) For the purposes of this section any notice to a personal representative, trustee in bankruptcy, receiver, administrator, liquidator or person acting otherwise as aforesaid shall be deemed to be notice to the person in respect of whom he so acts.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Certificates in cases of acquisitions of certain goods by a non-taxable person
49A.-(1) When a person who has, at a time when he was not a taxable person, acquires in the Republic any goods from another member state that are subject to excise duty or relate to new means of transport and -
(a) no notice of such acquisition has been served on the Commissioner, either before or after the date of commencement of this Act, by the person required by regulations made under paragraph 1(2B) of the Tenth Schedule.
(b) the Commissioner is not satisfied that the particulars relating to the acquisition in any notice served on him are accurate and complete; or
(c) there is a failure to supply the Commissioner with the necessary information to confirm the particulars contained in any such notice;
then the Registrar may, at his best discretion, certify this amount as VAT. due upon acquisition and to communicate the certificate to that person.
(2) When any amount has been ascertained and notified to any person pursuant to this article, then this shall be considered, subject to the provisions of this Law for objections, as a VAT amount. due from such person and may be recovered accordingly, unless, except to the extent that, the certificate has been subsequently revoked or amended by reducing the amount.
(3) For the purposes of this section any notice to a personal representative, receiver, receiver, administrator, liquidator or person otherwise acting in a representative capacity in relation to the person making the acquisition in question shall be deemed to be notice to the person in relation to whom he is acting.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Prescription and additional certifications
50.—(1) Subject to the following provisions of this section, a certificate under section 49 or 49A shall not be issued after the expiration of six years from the end of the specified tax period or importation or acquisition to which it relates.
(2) Subject to subsection (3) below, if VAT has been lost. as a result of fraud or willful omission on the part of the taxable person, then a certificate may be issued as if the reference to six years in subsection (1) were a reference to twelve years.
(3) If the Commissioner considers that the amount required to be certified under section 49 or 49A exceeds the amount certified, then, on or before the last day on which the certificate could be issued, the Commissioner may issue a supplementary certificate for the additional amount and notify the person concerned of the amount.
(4) Regardless of the provisions of the above subsections of this article, if—
(a) Tax certificate issued pursuant to article 49 or 49A; or
(b) a decision of the Tax Council concerning an objection to a tax certificate issued pursuant to article 49 or 49A, is declared invalid in whole or in part by the Administrative Court or by the Supreme Court on appeal pursuant to Article 146 of the Constitution and there is a need for review, the Commissioner may issue a new certificate within six months from the date the Court's decision was issued.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 86(I)/2017
 
 
 
 
 122(I)/2020)*

## Recovery of unduly paid VAT
51.—(1) If any person has (either before or after the coming into force of this Act) paid an amount to the Commissioner as VAT. which was not VAT due, then the Superintendent is bound to refund the said amount to the person.
(2) The Commissioner is obliged to return an amount under this article only if a relevant claim is submitted.
(3) The Commissioner may refuse to satisfy a claim pursuant to this article if the return of any amount would unjustifiably enrich the person submitting the claim.
(4) No amount may be claimed under this section after the expiration of three years from the date on which it was paid, except in cases where subsection (5) below applies.
(5) Where an amount has been paid to the Commissioner in error, a claim for the refund of the amount under this section may be made at any time before the expiration of three years from the date on which the person making the claim discovered the error or could with reasonable diligence have discovered it.
(6) Any claim pursuant to this article shall be submitted in the form and in the manner determined by the Council of Ministers by Regulations and shall be supported by written evidence; Regulations pursuant to this subsection may provide differently for different cases.
(7) Except as provided in this article, the Commissioner is not obliged to refund an amount paid to him as VAT. due to the fact that it was not VAT due to him.

*(95(I)/2000)*
51A.-(1) An objection may be submitted to the Commissioner regarding any amount of VAT. which has been verified and notified to any person pursuant to the provisions of this Law, and this shall not be considered if it is submitted after sixty (60) days from the date of notification of the VAT amount. which has been confirmed.
(2) Where the Commissioner accepts in part or rejects an objection made under subsection (1), his decision on the objection shall expressly include the grounds on which he relied and shall inform the person who made the objection—
(a) for the right of -
(i) To submit a hierarchical appeal to the Tax Board; or
(ii) to submit an appeal to the Administrative Court, in accordance with Article 146 of the Constitution, in which case the provisions of Article 52 shall apply mutatis mutandis.
(b) for time frames within which the aforementioned rights may be exercised.

*(95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 86(I)/2017)*

## Hierarchical appeal to the Tax Council
52.-(1) A taxable person, before submitting an appeal to the Administrative Court, may submit a hierarchical appeal to the Tax Council which is established based on the provisions of Article 4A of the Tax Certification and Collection Law, which must be accompanied by evidence supporting its validity, regarding the following issues:
(a) Amount of VAT imposed. on the delivery of any goods or the provision of any services, on the acquisition of goods from another Member State or on the importation of goods from outside the Member States;
(b) the amount of any input tax which may be deducted by any person;
(c) VAT certificate. by virtue of the provisions of Articles 49 and 49A;
(d) issuing a tax certificate pursuant to the provisions of article 50;
(e) any directive or supplementary directive issued under paragraph 2 of Part I of the First Schedule;
(f) any decision made under paragraph 1 of Part I of the Fourth Schedule.
(2) A Hierarchical Appeal based on the provisions of this article shall be submitted within forty-five (45) days from the date of notification to the applicant of the relevant decision or action of the Tax Commissioner and the period of forty-five (45) days may be extended for a reasonable time in the event that the Tax Council is satisfied, following a written application submitted by the applicant accompanied by complete supporting information, that the non-timely registration of the hierarchical appeal is due to the absence of the applicant from the Republic, his illness or another reasonable cause.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004
 
 
 
 
 86(I)/2017)*

## Submit an objection to the Registrar
52A.-(1) A taxable person may submit an objection to the Commissioner regarding the following issues:
(a) The registration or cancellation of registration of any person pursuant to the provisions of this Law;
(b) the inclusion, cancellation of inclusion or stay of any person in the Special Status of Farmers;
(c) any tax refund claim in accordance with Regulations issued pursuant to the provisions of article 12D;
(d) the allowable proportion of input tax under the provisions of section 21;
(e) a claim from a taxable person pursuant to the provisions of article 22;
(f) tax refund claim under the provisions of article 27 or article 42;
(g) the amount of any refunds under the provisions of section 30A;
(h) any rejection of an application under the provisions of section 32;
(i) the service of notification pursuant to the provisions of article 32;
(j) the requirement to lodge a bond under the provisions of section 37 or paragraph 3(2) of the Tenth Schedule;
(k) a claim for the refund of an amount pursuant to the provisions of article 51;
(l) determining the transaction value by a method described in a notice published under paragraph 1(3) of the Tenth Schedule;
(m) any refusal of authorization in relation to the plan made under Regulations made under paragraph 1(4) of the Tenth Schedule;
(n) any requirements imposed by the Commissioner in a particular case under paragraph 2 (2) (b) of the Tenth Schedule;
(o) any imposition of additional tax, pecuniary charge or interest under the provisions of articles 45, 45A and 45B.
(2) The objection to the Commissioner shall be submitted within sixty (60) days from the date of notification of the Commissioner's decision to the taxable person in relation to the matter raised in the objection.

*(86(I)/2017
 
 
 
 
 122(I)/2020)*

## Conditions for exercising a hierarchical appeal to the Tax Council
53. The Tax Council proceeds with the examination of a submitted hierarchical appeal only if the applicant-
(a) Has submitted the necessary documents and evidence to support its validity;
(b) has filed all tax returns required under paragraph 1(1) of the Tenth Schedule and has paid or made arrangements in respect of the amounts shown in those returns as payable by him;
(c) has paid the undisputed amount, has provided for the settlement in respect of it or has provided a similar guarantee to the Fiscal Council if this has been required by the Fiscal Council in the event that the hierarchical appeal is submitted against a decision on a matter provided for in article 52:
Provided that, in the event of a compromise being reached pursuant to the provisions of this Law or Regulations issued pursuant to it, there is no right to submit a hierarchical appeal:
It is further provided that, a hierarchical appeal which the Tax Council deems unfounded, pursuant to the provisions of this article, is summarily examined and rejected, without calling the applicant or the Commissioner before the Tax Council.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 86(I)/2017)*

## Appeals
54. If, as a result of an annulment decision of the Administrative Court or the Supreme Court in an appeal based on article 146 of the Constitution, the Commissioner must pay or return to a person a certain amount of money, including the amount he collected based on article 53(3), then he shall return or pay said amount with interest at nine percent (9%) per annum.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 86(I)/2017
 
 
 
 
 122(I)/2020)*

## Procedure for submitting a hierarchical appeal to the Tax Council
54A.-(1) The Fiscal Council shall notify the Registrar of the submitted hierarchical appeal and require the submission of a report and any information it deems necessary within a specially set deadline, which may not exceed three (3) months.
(2) The Fiscal Council sets a date for the hearing of the hierarchical appeal and by written notice invites the applicant or his representative and the Registrar or his representative to appear before it and state their views regarding any issue related to the hierarchical appeal under consideration.
(3) During the hearing of the hierarchical appeal, neither the Registrar nor the applicant have the right to present reasons or evidence that were not brought forward during the examination of the applicant's objection by the Registrar or when the administrative act was issued, unless such evidence is established that for objective reasons it was not possible to be presented by the applicant at that stage or to be revealed after an investigation by the Registrar that is reasonable in the circumstances.
(4) After the conclusion of the examination of the hierarchical appeal, the Tax Council, based on the data submitted to it, may issue one of the following decisions:
(a) To annul or validate in whole or in part the decision of the Commissioner;
(b) to modify the decision of the Commissioner;
(c) to issue a new decision to replace the decision of the Commissioner;
(d) refer the case to the Commissioner with instructions to him to take specific action.
(5) The procedure for examining the hierarchical appeal is completed as quickly as possible and the decision of the Tax Council is issued no later than one year from the date of its submission.
(6) In case there is a need to issue a new administrative act as a result of the decision of the Tax Council in order to implement the decision to comply with the directives of the Tax Council, this new administrative act shall be issued within sixty (60) days from the day of issuing the decision of the Tax Council.

*(86(I)/2017
 
 
 
 
 122(I)/2020)*

## Transactions that extend after the rate change, etc
55.—(1) This article applies when there is a change in the VAT rate. applicable under Articles 17 or 18 or in the description of exempted, zero-rated or reduced-rated acquisitions.
(2) When—
(a) a transaction affected by the change would, without regard to section 9(4), (5), (6) or (10), be deemed by virtue of section 9(2) or (3) to have taken place in whole or in part at a time which would not have been affected by the change; or
(b) a transaction not affected by the change would, without regard to section 9(4), (5), (6) or (10), be deemed, by virtue of section 9(2) or (3), to take place wholly or in part at a time in which the rate at which VAT is chargeable would be so affected. on the transaction, or any question as to whether the transaction is exempt or subject to the zero rate or the reduced rate, is determined, if the person making it so elects, without regard to section 9(4), (5), (6) or (10).
(3) Regulations made under this Act in relation to the time a transaction is deemed to take place may provide that this section applies as if the references in subsection (2) above to section 9(4), (5), (6) or (10) included references to specified provisions of the Regulations.
(3A) When -
(a) any acquisition of goods from another Member State which is affected by the change would not be affected, in whole or in part, if it had been deemed to have taken place at the time of the event which, in relation to that acquisition, is the first relevant event for the purposes of taxation of the acquisition. or
(b) any acquisition of goods from another Member State which is not affected by the change would be affected, in whole or in part, if it had been deemed to have taken place at the time of that event;
the rate based on which VAT is imposed on the acquisition or any question whether the acquisition is exempt or subject to the zero rate or reduced rate, is determined, if the person making the acquisition so elects, at the time of that event.
(4) Regulations issued pursuant to paragraph 1 of the Tenth Schedule may provide for the replacement or correction of any VAT invoice. which—
(a) It relates to a transaction in respect of which an election is made under this section, but
(b) was issued before the election was made.
(5) No election may be made under this section in relation to a transaction to which section 9(9) or paragraph 5 of the Second Schedule applies.
(6) References in this article to an acquisition subject to the zero rate are references to the acquisition of goods from another member state which is the one in relation to which article 25(3) provides that no VAT is imposed.
(7) References to this Article -
(a) in a transaction which is a transaction subject to the reduced rate; or
(b) in an acquisition which is an acquisition subject to the reduced rate,
are references with a transaction or, depending on the case, an acquisition, which is the one on which VAT is imposed. with the rate applicable pursuant to article 18.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Adjustment of contracts
56.—(1) When, after the conclusion of a contract for the delivery of goods or the provision of services and before the goods are delivered or the services are rendered, there is a change in the VAT. imposed on the transaction, then, unless the contract provides otherwise, an amount equal to the change shall be added to or deducted from the consideration.
(2) References of this article to a change in VAT. imposed on a transaction include references to a change involving the imposition or non-imposition of VAT. on the transaction.

*(95(I)/2000)*

## Delivery of Notices
57. Any notice, demand, decision, instruction or other act to be served on any person for the purposes of this Law may be served by letter addressed to that person or to his representative for VAT purposes. and which is sent by registered letter by post to the last declared or usual residence or place of business of that person or his representative or delivered personally to the person to whom it is addressed or his representative.

*(95(I)/2000)*

## Financial charges based on Regulations
58. Subject to the provisions of article 45(5)(f), any Regulations issued by the Council of Ministers under this Law may contain provisions for the imposition of a fine of £50 on persons who violate the provisions of these Regulations.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004)*

## Reservations and transitional provisions, and repeals
59.—(1) The Value Added Tax Law of 1990 or any articles thereof shall be repealed by a decision of the Council of Ministers, which shall be published in the Official Gazette of the Republic.
(2) The continuity of legislation related to VAT is not affected by the replacement of the repealed Law by this Law.
(3) Any person who, immediately before the effective date of this Law, was, or is deemed to have been, a taxable person, continues to maintain this status even after the date in question and is considered to be registered in the VAT Register. based on the provisions of this Law.
(4) If the tax period of any person subject to tax began before and will be completed after the date of entry into force of this Law, then any matter concerning the tax period in question shall be regulated based on the provisions of the repealed Law as regards the part of the tax period that preceded the date in question.
(5) Entries in the VAT Register for the purposes of Part II and Part III of the First Schedule made before the commencement of this Act, shall have effect as if made under the provisions of Part II and Part III of the First Schedule to this Act.
(6) The approval of any person as a VAT custodian. and the approval of any place as a VAT warehouse. by the Registrar for the purposes of article 13B made before the entry into force of this Law, will be valid as if made by virtue of the provisions of article 13B of this Law.
(7) In compliance with the provisions of the legislation governing state aid as well as de minimis aid, the Minister of Finance, following an application by a beneficiary submitted to him or to a person authorized by him and following a proposal by the Commissioner and a decision of the Council of Ministers, may write off any amount of an additional charge imposed or any amount of interest imposed pursuant to article 38 or any monetary charge imposed pursuant to of subsection (14) of article 25 of the 1990 Law, when the beneficiary has paid before the entry into force of this subsection or undertakes to pay the amount of tax due under the provisions of the 1990 Law, within the time limit set by the Minister of Finance or a person authorized by him, observing the following:
(a) For persons whose registration has been canceled due to the fact that they have ceased to carry out any business and continue for a period of three years from this time, not to carry out any business, the entire amount of additional charge imposed or imposed interest pursuant to article 38 or monetary charge imposed pursuant to subsection (14) of article 25 of the 1990 Law reduced by the flat rate shall be deleted amount of two hundred euros (€200) or if this is less than two hundred euros (€200), then they must pay this:
It is understood that the cessation of business does not cover the cases where it was the result of its transfer as an active economic unit without substantially changing its ownership status.
(b) For persons who still remain registered in the VAT Register. or the cancellation of their registration is not covered by paragraph (a), any deletion of the amount of additional charge imposed or the amount of interest imposed pursuant to article 38 or of a monetary charge imposed pursuant to subsection (14) of article 25 of the 1990 Law, is governed by Regulation (EC) no. 1998/2006 of the Commission of 15her December 2006, Regulation (EC) no. 1535/2007 of the Commission of 20her of December 2007 and Regulation (EC) no. 875/2007 of the Commission of 24her July 2007 and in such a case, with the application submitted to the Minister of Finance or to a person authorized by him, the written statement required under the Control of State Aids (Minor Aids) Regulations of 2009 and 2012, as amended or replaced from time to time, shall also be submitted.
(c) Persons to whom paragraph (b) applies and who undertake to pay the tax due, must additionally pay the part of the due amount of an additional charge imposed or an amount of interest imposed by virtue of article 38 or of a financial charge imposed by virtue of subsection (14) of article 25 of the Law of 1990, which corresponds to a percentage of ten percent (10%) on the amount of tax due or if it is less than two hundred euros (€200), then they must pay the amount of two hundred euros (€200) that is not deleted.
(d) For persons to whom paragraph (b) applies and who have paid the tax before the entry into force of this subsection, any amount of imposed additional charge or imposed amount of interest pursuant to article 38 or imposed monetary charge pursuant to subsection (14) of article 25 of the Law of 1990 reduced by the amount of two hundred euros (€200), shall be deleted, with the payment of the amount of two hundred euros (€200) by the person in question only in the event that the amount of an additional charge imposed or an amount of interest imposed pursuant to article 38 or a monetary charge imposed pursuant to subsection (14) of article 25 of the 1990 Law, which may be deleted, exceeds the amount of two hundred euros (€200).
(e) A person is not entitled to submit an application under the provisions of paragraph (b), when the amount of additional monetary burden and interest imposed on the basis of article 38 and monetary burden imposed on the basis of subsection (14) of article 25 of the 1990 Law, reduced by the amount corresponding to the percentage of ten percent (10%) on the amount of the tax or, depending on the case, by the amount of two hundred euros (€200), is greater than the maximum limits specified in the Control of State Aids (Minimum Aids) Regulations of 2009 and 2012, as they are amended or replaced from time to time:
Provided that the Registrar does not submit a proposal to the Minister of Finance pursuant to the provisions of this subsection when the application is related to a troubled business.
For the purposes of this subsection, a company is problematic if it is not capable, with its own financial resources or with resources it can secure from its owners or shareholders and its creditors, to stop its loss-making course and a person who still has VAT. due based on the provisions of this Law is considered to be running a problem business, if the debt in question has become overdue.
(8) For the purposes of subsection (7), when the amount for which a request for reduction or cancellation is submitted by a beneficiary person or a person authorized by him does not exceed twenty thousand euros (€20,000), a decision of the Council of Ministers is not required, but a decision of the Minister of Finance, following a proposal by the Treasurer.

*(95(I)/2000
 
 
 
 
 95(I)/2004

 172(I)/2012
 
 
 
 
 154(I)/2014)*

## Transitional measures in relation to the accession of the Republic to the European Union
59A.-(1) Regardless of the provisions of this Law, when goods:
(a) They have entered the territory of the Republic before May 1, 2004; and
(b) upon entering the territory of the Republic have been subject to -
(i) bonded warehouses, bonded warehouses or free zone;
(ii) temporary importation or transshipment status; and
(c) have been admitted to the territorial waters of the Republic-
(i) to be incorporated into drilling or exploitation rigs, for the purpose of constructing, repairing, maintaining, retrofitting them or their equipment or for connecting the rigs to land, or
(ii) to supply such drilling or exploitation rigs; and
(d) have not exited the above regimes before 1 May 2004;
the provisions in force at the time of their entry into the above regimes continue to apply until the exit of the goods from said regimes after May 1, 2004.
(2) When goods -
(a) have been placed before 1 May 2004 under a common transit procedure or under another customs transit procedure and
(b) did not exit that status before 1 May 2004;
the provisions in force at the time the goods are placed under that procedure continue to apply until the goods leave that procedure after 1 May 2004.
(3) Simulated as importation of goods:
(a) subject to the provisions of subsection (1), the movement, even irregularly, of the goods from one of the regimes referred to in paragraphs (b) and (c) of that subsection or from a regime similar to those regimes and to which the goods were subject before 1 May 2004;
(b) the expiry of the regime referred to in subsection (2) which began before 1 May 2004, for the purposes of a supply of goods for consideration made before that date within the Republic by a taxable person acting in that capacity;
(c) any irregularity or offense committed while the goods are in the regime referred to in subsection (2), subject to the terms of paragraph (b).
The importation is considered to take place in the Republic when the goods leave the regime to which they were subject before May 1, 2004 on the territory of the Republic.
(4) It is assimilated to the importation of goods or any use after May 1, 2004, within the Republic, by a taxable or non-taxable person, of goods delivered to him before May 1, 2004, within the European Union or within one of the new member states, when the following conditions are met -
(a) the delivery of the goods had been exempted or may be exempted under similar provisions in force in the Member States and in the new Member States to those of the provisions of Article 25 (6), (7) and (8);
(b) the goods were not imported into the Republic before 1 May 2004;
(5) No VAT is imposed.
(a) on imported goods which are dispatched or transported outside the enlarged European Union; or
(b) on imported goods placed under temporary importation within the meaning of subsection (3)(a) which are not means of transport, and are re-dispatched or transported to the Member State from which they were exported and by the person who exported them; or
(c) on means of transport which are under a temporary import regime within the meaning of subsection (3)(a) which were acquired or imported before 1 May 2004 under the general taxation conditions of the internal market of one of the new Member States or one of the Member States and/or did not qualify due to their exportation, exemption or refund of value added tax. This condition is deemed to be met when the date of first entry into circulation of the means of transport is earlier than May 1, 1996 or when the amount of import tax due is negligible.
For the purposes of this article –
"new Member States" means the territory of the Member States which accede to the European Union on 1 May 2004;
"enlarged European Union" means the territory of the European Union after 1 May 2004.

*(95(I)/2004)*

## Effective date
60.—(1) This Law or any of its articles shall come into force on a date to be determined by a decision of the Council of Ministers, or which shall be published in the Official Gazette of the Republic.
(2) Regardless of the provisions of subsection (1), the provisions of this Law which provide for the issuance of Regulations, apply from the date of publication of this Law in the official Gazette of the Republic, only for purposes of issuing the relevant Regulations.

*(95(I)/2000)*

## Special provisions
61. -(1) The provisions of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 3) Law of 2017 come into force from the date of its publication in the Official Gazette of the Republic, with the exception of the provisions-
(a) Article 2 thereof, which shall enter into force on January 2, 2018 and shall cease to apply on the expiry date of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) Law of 2015, in accordance with the provisions of Article 3 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 2) Law of 2017.
(b) of paragraph (b) of this article 3, which enter into force on January 2, 2018.
(2) Regardless of the provisions of article 4 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) Law of 2018 [S.S.: i.e. of Law 39(I)/2018], the provisions of articles 2 and 3 thereof shall come into force on 1h January 2019.
(3) Regardless of the provisions of article 4 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) Law of 2015, article 3 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 2) Law of 2017 and paragraph (a) of subsection (1)-
(a) the provisions of articles 2 and 3 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) Law of 2015 are deemed to apply from 1 January 2020 until 31 December 2026. and
(b) the provisions of article 2 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 3) Law of 2017 are deemed to apply from January 1, 2020 until December 31, 2026.

## Retroactive effect of Regulations
62. Regulations issued under articles 3B, 28(1), Regulations. 28(3), 42B, 42F and 42H for purposes of harmonization with European Union law may have retroactive effect.

*(181(I)/2021)*

## Transitional provisions in relation to the application of the provisions of Part C
63. The provisions of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) Law of 2023 do not apply to buildings for which planning permission has been secured by the competent authority or an application for planning permission has been submitted to the competent authority until 31h October 2023 and for which the duly completed declaration provided for in the fourth subparagraph of paragraph (3) of Table C of the Fifth Appendix is submitted, within three (3) years from the date of entry into force of said law:
It is provided that the Tax Commissioner may examine applications submitted pursuant to this article, until 31h December 2026, the examination of which was not completed in time due to the delay of the urban planning authorities.

*(42(I)/2023
 
 
 
 
 109(I)/2026)*

## Special provisions regarding the special sponsorship for the purchase or construction of a residence
64.-(1) Subject to the provisions of subsections (2) and (3), the provisions of subsections (2) and (3) of article 5 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 3) Law of 2011 are repealed six (6) months after the date of entry into force of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 2) Law of 2023:
Provided that, applications which have been submitted and are under consideration before the date of entry into force of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 2) Law of 2023 or have been submitted within the period of six (6) months provided for in subsection (1), are examined and completed in accordance with the provisions of the Special Sponsorship (Purchase and Construction of Residence) Law, which was partially repealed by the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 3) Law of 2011, which, for this purpose, are considered to be still in force.
(2) In the event that an application, to which the provisions of the proviso in subsection (1) apply and which has been submitted pursuant to the provisions of subsections (2) and (3) of article 5 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 3) Law of 2011, is incomplete and/or information or documents are missing in relation to it, the applicant is informed accordingly and his application is not considered complete for examination and decision purposes until he submits the required information.
(3) In case of submission of an incomplete application and/or incomplete documents and/or information accompanying the application, the applicant is informed within a regular deadline, determined by the competent authority, of the need to correct the application and/or submit the required documents and/or information and proceed to correct the application and/or submit the required documents and/or information within a regular deadline determined by the competent authority:
Provided that, in case of non-compliance by the applicant within the above deadline, his application may be rejected without the possibility of re-submitting an application for the same purpose.
(4) The provisions of article 9 of the Special Sponsorship (Purchase and Construction of Residence) Law continue to apply in relation to persons who have received special sponsorship under the provisions of the Special Sponsorship (Purchase and Construction of Residence) Law and subsections (2) and (3) of article 5 of the Value Added Tax (Amendment) (No. 3) Law of 2011.

*(63(I)/2023)*
FIRST APPENDIX
Articles 6, 10(5), 11B, 11C, 11D, 11E, 12E(1) and (2), 42(1), 45(5) and 59(5)
PART I:
Section 1
REGISTRATION IN RELATION TO TAXABLE TRANSACTIONS
Obligation to register
1.—(1) Subject to the provisions of subsections (3) to (5) below, a person who carries out taxable transactions and is not registered under this Act shall be required to register—
(a) At the end of any month, if the person is resident in the Republic and the value of his chargeable transactions during the period of one year ending at that point in time has exceeded £9,000, or
(b) at any time if there are reasonable grounds for believing that the value of his chargeable transactions during the thirty day period beginning with that time will exceed £9,000.
(2) When a business carried on by a taxable person is transferred to another as an operating economic unit and the transferee is not registered under this Law at the time of the transfer, then, subject to the provisions of subsections (3) to (5) below, the transferee becomes liable to register under this Part at the time of the transfer if—
(a) The value of his chargeable transactions during the one-year period ending at the time of the transfer has exceeded £9,000, or
(b) there are reasonable grounds for believing that the value of his chargeable transactions during the thirty day period beginning with the time of the transfer will exceed £9,000.
(3) A person shall not be liable to register under subsection (1)(a) or (2)(a) above if the Commissioner is satisfied that the value of his chargeable transactions during the period of one year beginning with the time at which, without regard to this subsection, he would have been required to register, will not exceed £8,000.
(3A) For determining the value of transactions of a person for the purposes of paragraph 1(a) and 2(a), transactions carried out at a time when it was previously registered under this Law are ignored if -
(a) his writ was canceled for reasons other than those referred to in paragraph 13(3), paragraph 6(2) of Part II, or paragraph 6(3) of Part III of this Schedule, and
(b) the Registrar is satisfied that before the registration was cancelled, he was given all the information necessary to determine whether to cancel the registration.
(3B) A person shall be deemed to have become liable for registration under this Part at any time when such obligation arose in accordance with the previous provisions of this paragraph except for any registration which is later canceled under paragraph 13(3), paragraph 6(2) of Part II or paragraph 6(3) of Part III of this Schedule.
(4) A person does not cease to be liable to register under this Part except in accordance with paragraph 2(5), 3 or 4 below.
(5) When determining the value of person transactions for the purposes of subsection (1) or (2) above, supplies of goods or services, which are capital assets of the business within the framework of or for the promotion of which they are delivered or provided and any taxable transactions which would not be taxable transactions without regard to article 10(4), shall not be taken into account.
(6) In determining the value of a person's transactions for the purposes of sub-paragraphs (1) or (2), transactions to which section 13B(4) applies and transactions deemed to have been carried out by him by virtue of section 13C(3) shall be disregarded.
(7) A person is considered "established in the Republic" when he has a business establishment or other permanent establishment in the Republic in relation to the business he carries out.
(1A) Notwithstanding the provisions of this Schedule and any other provisions of this Act applicable for the purposes of registration, any person who makes or intends to make taxable supplies of citrus fruits, and all his supplies are subject to the zero rate (0%), shall not be liable to register:
It is provided that any person who does not become liable for registration by virtue of the immediately preceding paragraph and satisfies the Registrar that he carries out or intends to carry out taxable deliveries of citrus fruits, may request in writing to be registered pursuant to the provisions of the Value Added Tax Law and the Registrar, if he deems it appropriate, shall proceed with his registration, which shall take effect from the day he requested it:
Provided further that for the purposes of this paragraph, the term "citrus" includes lemons, oranges, tangerines, grapefruit and citrus.
2.—(1) Without prejudice to the provisions of paragraph 1 above and in order to prevent the maintenance or creation of any artificial separation of the business activities carried on by two or more persons resulting in the avoidance of payment of VAT, if the Commissioner issues a directive pursuant to this paragraph, the persons named in the directive shall be considered as a single subject to the tax a person carrying out the activities of the business described in the directive and that the taxable person is required to register under this Part; his obligation to register begins on the date of the directive or, if the directive so provides, from such later date as may be specified in the directive.
(2) The Commissioner shall not issue a direction under this paragraph naming any person unless he is satisfied—
(a) That such person carries out or has carried out taxable transactions; and
(b) that the activities within the context of which he carries out or has carried out the taxable transactions in question form part only of certain activities which should normally be regarded as the activities of the undertaking described in the directive, and that the other activities are carried on at the same time or were previously carried on (or both) by one or more other persons; and
(c) that, if all the taxable transactions of that business were taken into account, the person carrying on that business would be liable to be registered under paragraph 1 above at the time the direction is made; and
(d) that the main reason or one of the main reasons that the person in question carries on the activities mentioned first in paragraph (b) above in the manner in which he carries them on is to avoid the obligation to register (whether the obligation would be his, any other person's or two or more persons' jointly).
(3) A directive issued pursuant to this paragraph shall be served on each of the persons named therein.
(4) When the Commissioner finds that a person who was not named in a directive issued pursuant to this paragraph carries out taxable transactions within the framework of activities that should normally have been considered as part of the activities of the enterprise described in the directive, the Commissioner may issue and serve on that person a supplementary directive which refers to the previous directive and to the enterprise specified therein and to which his name is added of the person to those of the persons named in the previous directive; the validity of the supplementary directive begins from—
(a) The date on which he began to carry out the taxable transactions in question, or
(b) if later, the date from which the single taxable person referred to in the previous directive became liable to register under this Part.
(5) If, immediately before a direction (including a supplementary direction) made under this paragraph, any person named in the direction is registered in relation to taxable transactions carried on as referred to in sub-paragraph (2) or (4) above, he ceases to be liable to register; his obligation to register ceases on the later of any of the following dates—
(a) The date from which the single taxable person in question became liable to register; and
(b) the date of the directive.
(6) In relation to an enterprise specified in a directive or a supplementary directive issued under this paragraph, the persons named in the directive together with any person named in the supplementary directive who is connected with that enterprise (being taken together as a single taxable person) are referred to in sub-paragraphs (7) and (8) below as the "founding members".
(7) Where a direction is issued under this paragraph, then, for the purposes of this Act—
(a) The taxable person carrying on the business specified in the direction is registrable by such name as the persons named in the direction jointly wish to indicate by written notice given to the Commissioner within fourteen days of the date of the direction or, if they fail to indicate any name, by the name specified in the direction;
(b) any supply of goods or provision of services by or to one of the founding members in the course of the activities of the taxable person shall be deemed to be a transaction by or to that person;
(ba) any acquisition of goods from another Member State by one of the founding members in the course of the activities of the taxable person shall be deemed to be an acquisition by that person;
(c) each of the founding members is jointly and severally liable for any VAT. owed by the taxable person;
(d) without prejudice to paragraph (c) above, any failure of the taxable person to comply with any obligation imposed under this Law shall be deemed to be the failure of each founding member separately; and
(e) subject to the provisions of paragraphs (a) to (d) above, the founding members shall be deemed to be a partnership carrying on the business of the taxable person and any question as to the extent of the activities of that business at any time shall be decided accordingly.
(8) If the Commissioner finds that any person who is one of the founding members should no longer be regarded as such for the purposes of paragraphs (c) and (d) of subsection (7) above and gives notice that he does not consider him to be a founding member, that person shall not be liable under the said paragraphs for anything done after the date specified in the notice and, accordingly, that date shall be deemed to have been ceases to be a member of the cooperative referred to in paragraph (e) of subsection (7).
(9) In determining for the purposes of subsection (1) above whether any separation of business activities is artificial, regard shall be had to the extent to which the different persons carrying on those activities are closely connected with each other by financial, economic and organizational ties.
3. A person who has become liable to register under this Part ceases to be liable to register at any time if the Commissioner is satisfied in relation to that time that the person in question—
(a) Ceased to carry out taxable transactions; or
(b) is not at the time a person in respect of whom any of the conditions set out in paragraphs 1(1)(a) and (b) and (2)(a) and (b) above apply.
4.—(1) Subject to the provisions of subsection (2) below, a person who has become liable to be registered under this Part shall cease to be liable to be registered at any time after his registration if the Commissioner is satisfied that the value of his chargeable transactions during the period of one year commencing then will not exceed £8,000.
(2) The person does not cease to be liable to be registered under subsection (1) above if the Commissioner is satisfied that the reason why the value of his taxable transactions will not exceed £8,000 is that during the said period he will cease to carry out taxable transactions, or will suspend their carrying on for a period of thirty days or more.
(3) When determining the value of a person's transactions for the purposes of subsection (1) above, supplies of goods or services, which are capital assets of the business in the context of or in furtherance of which they are delivered or provided and any taxable transactions which would not be taxable transactions but for section 10(4) shall be disregarded.
Notice of Registration Obligation and Registration
5.—(1) A person who becomes obliged to register by virtue of paragraph 1(1)(a) above must notify the Registrar of his obligation within thirty days of the end of the relevant month.
(2) The Registrar shall register any such person (whether or not he notifies the Registrar) and his registration shall take effect from the end of the month following the relevant month or from such earlier date as may be agreed between the Registrar and such person.
(3) In this paragraph "relevant month", in relation to a person who becomes liable to register under paragraph 1(1)(a) above, means the month at the end of which he becomes liable to register.
6.—(1) A person who becomes liable to register by virtue of paragraph 1(1)(b) above must notify the Registrar of his obligation before the end of the period of thirty days within which the obligation arises.
(2) The Registrar shall register any such person (whether or not he notifies the Registrar) and his registration shall take effect from the beginning of the period of thirty days within which the liability arises.
7.—(1) A person who becomes obliged to register by virtue of paragraph 1(2) above must notify the Registrar of his obligation within thirty days from the time of the transfer of the business.
(2) The Registrar shall register any such person (whether or not he notifies the Registrar) and his registration shall take effect from the time of the transfer of the business.
8. Where a person becomes liable to register under paragraph 1(1)(a) and under paragraph 1(1)(b) or 1(2) at the same time, the Registrar shall register him under paragraph 6(2) or 7(2) above, as the case may be, instead of under the provisions of paragraph 5(2) above.
Right to registration
9. Where a person, who is not required to register under this Act and is not already registered, satisfies the Registrar that—
(a) Makes taxable transactions; or
(b) carries on a business and intends to carry on such transactions in the course of or in furtherance of such business;
the Registrar, if the person in question requests it in writing, registers him and the registration is valid from the day he requested it or from an earlier date that could be agreed between the Registrar and that person.
10.—(1) When a person, who is not required to register under this Act and is not already registered, satisfies the Registrar that-
(a) Conducts transactions falling within subsection (2) below; or
(b) carries on a business and intends to carry on such transactions in the course of or in furtherance of that business, and (in either case) falls within subsection (3) below, the Commissioner shall, if requested in writing by such person, register it and his registration shall take effect from the day on which he requests it or from such earlier date as may be agreed between the Commissioner and such person.
(2) A transaction falls within this subsection if—
(a) It is carried out outside the Republic or if it falls under the provisions of article 21(2)(c) of this Law, but it would be a taxable transaction if it were carried out inside the Republic,
(b) falls under the provisions of article 42 A of the Law.
(3) A person comes within this subsection if—
(a) He has a business establishment in the Republic or his usual place of residence is in the Republic; and
(b) does not carry out and does not intend to carry out taxable transactions.
(4) For the purposes of this paragraph—
(a) A person carrying on business through a branch or agency in the Republic shall be deemed to have a place of business in the Republic, and
(b) "ordinary place of residence", in relation to a body corporate, means the place where it is legally incorporated.
Notice of Termination of Obligation or Right to Register
11. A person registered by virtue of paragraph 5, 6 or 9 above, who ceases to carry out or intends to carry out taxable transactions, must notify the Registrar of this fact within sixty days from the day of its effect unless, when he ceases in this way, he would otherwise be liable or entitled to registration under this Law if his registration and any legislation which prevents a person from being liable for registration under different provisions at the same time were not taken into account.
12. A person registered under paragraph 10 above who—
(a) Ceases to carry on or intend to carry on transactions falling within sub-paragraph (2) of paragraph 10; or
(b) carries out or intends to carry out taxable transactions,
must notify the Registrar of such fact within sixty days from the day of his departure unless, in the case of a person who ceases under sub-paragraph (a), he would, when so ceasing, otherwise be liable or entitled to register under this Act if his registration and any legislation preventing a person from being liable to register under different provisions at the same time were not taken into account.
Cancellation of registration
13.—(1) Where a registered person satisfies the Registrar that he is not required to be registered under this Part, the Registrar shall, if the person so requests in writing, cancel his registration and the cancellation shall take effect from the day on which he so requests or from such later date as may be agreed between the Registrar and that person.
(2) Where the Registrar is satisfied that a registered person has ceased to be registrable, he may cancel his registration and the cancellation shall have effect from the day of cessation or from such later date as may be agreed between the Registrar and such person.
(3) When the Registrar is satisfied that on the day on which a person was registered he was not eligible to register, he may cancel his registration and the cancellation shall be effective from that day.
(4) The Registrar shall not cancel under subsection (1) the registration of a person with the effect of canceling it from any time unless he is satisfied that it is not the time during which that person would be liable for registration under this Law.
(5) The Registrar shall not cancel by virtue of subsection (2) the registration of a person with the effect of canceling it from any time, unless he is satisfied that it is not the time in which that person would be liable, or entitled, to register under this Law.
(6) In determining for the purposes of sub-paragraphs (4) or (5) whether a person would be liable, or entitled, to register at any time shall not be taken into account, only to the extent of any provision of this Act or which prevents a person from becoming liable or entitled to register when he is already registered or when he is so liable by virtue of any other provision.
(7) In this paragraph, any reference to a registered person is a reference to a person who is registered under this Part.
Power to vary specified sums by ordinance
14. The Council of Ministers may by decree replace any of the amounts specified in this Part with other amounts it deems appropriate.
Supplementary provisions
15. The value of delivery of goods or provision of services is determined for the purposes of this Part without taking into account any VAT. enforceable on the transaction.
16. Any notice required to be given to the Commissioner under this Part shall be in such form and contain such particulars as Regulations made by the Cabinet may prescribe.
17. In this Schedule "registrable" means a person liable or entitled to register under this Part.
18. References in this Part to transactions are references to transactions carried out in the course of, or in furtherance of, the business of a business.
Section 2
REGISTRATION IN RELATION TO PERSONS ESTABLISHED IN THE REPUBLIC OF BENEFITED FROM THE SMALL BUSINESS REGIME IN OTHER MEMBER STATE
1. For the purposes of this Section-
"exempt small business" means any taxable person established within the territory of the European Union which carries out an annual turnover within the territory of the European Union which does not exceed one hundred thousand euros (€100,000) and which benefits from or wishes to and is entitled to benefit from an exemption in a member state in accordance with point 3 of this Section;
"annual turnover within the European Union" means the annual total value of taxable transactions excluding VAT, carried out by a taxable person within the territory of the European Union during a calendar year.
2. Subject to the provisions of Section 1 of Part I of this Annex, points 3 to 13 shall apply for the purposes of this Section.
3.(1) A taxable person who is established in the Republic to make use of the right to exemption in another member state, other than the Republic, based on provisions applicable in that member state corresponding to the provisions of Section 2 of Part IA-
(a) provides prior notification to the Commissioner of the exercise of the right of exemption in another Member State or States;
(b) informs the Registrar of his intention to make use of the option granted to another Member State or Member States for exemption from the obligation to register, due to his annual turnover;
(c) is identified for the implementation of the VAT number exemption in the Republic and bears the suffix “EX”;
(d) submit data on the value of taxable transactions expressed in euros that have taken place since the beginning of the current calendar quarter;
(e) informs about the termination of the exemption from the obligation to register in another member state or states and such termination:
Provided that, the suspension takes effect from the first day of the next calendar quarter after the receipt of the information by the taxable person or, in the event that such information is received during the last month of the calendar quarter, from the first day of the second month of the following calendar quarter.
(2) The notification submitted under the provisions of subparagraph (a) of paragraph (1) shall contain the information provided for in point 4 of this Section.
(3)(a) The exemption applies in respect of the Member State in which the taxable person is not established and in which the said person intends to make use of the exemption by virtue of-
(i) prior notification, from the date on which the individual identification number is communicated to the taxable person by the Member State of establishment; or
(ii) updating a previous notification, from the date on which the Member State of establishment confirms the number to the taxable person, as a consequence of the update.
(b) The date provided for in sub-paragraph (a) does not exceed thirty-five (35) working days from the receipt of the previous notification or the update of the previous notification provided for in sub-paragraphs (a) to (e) of paragraph (1) of point 2, except in special cases in which for the prevention of tax evasion or avoidance the Commissioner of Taxes may determine a longer period for carrying out the necessary controls.
4. (1) The prior notification provided for in subsection (a) of paragraph (1) of point 3 contains at least the following information:
(a) The name, activity, legal form and address of the taxable person;
(b) the Member State or Member States in which the taxable person intends to make use of the exemption;
(c) the total value of supplies of goods and/or services rendered in the Member State in which the taxable person is established and in each of the other Member States during the previous calendar year;
(d) the total value of supplies of goods and/or services that have taken place in the Member State in which the taxable person is established and in each of the other Member States during the current calendar year prior to notification.
(2) The information provided for in subparagraph (c) of paragraph (1) of point 4 is provided for each previous calendar year included in the period provided for in Article 288a, paragraph 1, first paragraph of Directive (EU) 2020/285 for each member state that applies the option provided for there.
(3) In a case where the taxable person informs the Member State of establishment, in accordance with subparagraph (a) of paragraph (1) of point 3, that he intends to make use of the exemption in a Member State or Member States other than those mentioned in the previous notification, the said person is not required to provide the information referred to in paragraph (1) of point 4, as long as the information in question has already been included in information reports submitted in a previous year under the point 5.
5.
